\chapter{Intel® Embree Overview}\label{intel-embree-overview}

Intel® Embree is a high-performance ray tracing library developed at
Intel, which is released as open source under the
\href{http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0}{Apache 2.0 license}.
Intel® Embree supports x86 CPUs under Linux, macOS, and Windows; ARM
CPUs on Linux and macOS; as well as Intel® GPUs under Linux and Windows.

Intel® Embree targets graphics application developers to improve the
performance of photo-realistic rendering applications. Embree is
optimized towards production rendering, by putting focus on incoherent
ray performance, high quality acceleration structure construction, a
rich feature set, accurate primitive intersection, and low memory
consumption.

Embree's feature set includes various primitive types such as triangles
(as well quad and grids for lower memory consumption); Catmull-Clark
subdivision surfaces; various types of curve primitives, such as flat
curves (for distant views), round curves (for closeup views), and normal
oriented curves, all supported with different basis functions (linear,
Bézier, B-spline, Hermite, and Catmull Rom); point-like primitives, such
as ray oriented discs, normal oriented discs, and spheres; user defined
geometries with a procedural intersection function; multi-level
instancing; filter callbacks invoked for any hit encountered; motion
blur including multi-segment motion blur, deformation blur, and
quaternion motion blur; and ray masking.

Intel® Embree contains ray tracing kernels optimized for the latest x86
processors with support for SSE, AVX, AVX2, and AVX-512 instructions,
and uses runtime code selection to choose between these kernels. Intel®
Embree contains algorithms optimized for incoherent workloads
(e.g.~Monte Carlo ray tracing algorithms) and coherent workloads
(e.g.~primary visibility and hard shadow rays) as well as supports for
dynamic scenes by implementing high-performance two-level spatial index
structure construction algorithms.

Intel® Embree supports applications written with the Intel® Implicit
SPMD Program Compiler (Intel® ISPC, \url{https://ispc.github.io/}) by
providing an ISPC interface to the core ray tracing algorithms. This
makes it possible to write a renderer that automatically vectorizes and
leverages SSE, AVX, AVX2, and AVX-512 instructions.

Intel® Embree supports Intel GPUs through the
\href{https://www.khronos.org/sycl/}{SYCL} open standard programming
language. SYCL allows to write C++ code that can be run on various
devices, such as CPUs and GPUs. Using Intel® Embree application
developers can write a single source renderer that executes efficiently
on CPUs and GPUs. Maintaining just one code base this way can
significantly improve productivity and eliminate inconsistencies between
a CPU and GPU version of the renderer. Embree supports GPUs based on the
Xe HPG and Xe HPC microarchitecture, which support hardware accelerated
ray tracing do deliver excellent levels of ray tracing performance.

\section{Supported Platforms}\label{supported-platforms}

Embree supports Windows (32-bit and 64-bit), Linux (64-bit), and macOS
(64-bit). Under Windows, Linux and macOS x86 based CPUs are supported,
while ARM CPUs are currently only supported under Linux and macOS (e.g.~
Apple M1). ARM support for Windows experimental.

Embree supports Intel GPUs based on the Xe HPG microarchitecture (Intel®
Arc™ GPU) under Linux and Windows and Xe HPC microarchitecture (Intel®
Data Center GPU Flex Series and Intel® Data Center GPU Max Series) under
Linux.

The code compiles with the Intel® Compiler, Intel® oneAPI DPC++
Compiler, GCC, Clang, and the Microsoft Compiler. To use Embree on the
GPU the Intel® oneAPI DPC++ Compiler must be used. Please see section
\hyperref[compiling-embree]{Compiling Embree} for details on tested
compiler versions.

Embree requires at least an x86 CPU with support for SSE2 or an Apple M1
CPU.

\section{Embree Support and Contact}\label{embree-support-and-contact}

If you encounter bugs please report them via
\href{https://github.com/embree/embree/issues}{Embree's GitHub Issue
Tracker}.

For questions and feature requests please write us at
\href{mailto:embree_support@intel.com}{\nolinkurl{embree\_support@intel.com}}.

To receive notifications of updates and new features of Embree please
subscribe to the \href{https://groups.google.com/d/forum/embree/}{Embree
mailing list}.

\section{Version History}\label{version-history}

\subsection{Embree 4.1.0}\label{embree-4.1.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added support for Intel® Data Center GPU Max Series.
\item
  Added ARM64 Linux support.
\item
  Added EMBREE\_BACKFACE\_CULLING\_SPHERES cmake option. The new cmake
  option defaults to OFF.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 4.0.1}\label{embree-4.0.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Improved performance for Tiger Lake, Comet Lake, Cannon Lake, Kaby
  Lake, and Skylake client CPUs by using 256 bit SIMD instructions by
  default.
\item
  Fixed broken motion blur of RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_ROUND\_LINEAR\_CURVE
  geometry type.
\item
  Fixed bvh build retry issue for TBB 2020.3
\item
  Added support for Intel® Data Center GPU Flex Series
\item
  Fixed issue on systems without a SYCL platform.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 4.0.0}\label{embree-4.0.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  This Embree release adds support for Intel® Arc™ GPUs through SYCL.
\item
  The SYCL support of Embree is in beta phase. Current functionality,
  quality, and GPU performance may not reflect that of the final
  product. Please read the documentation section ``Embree SYCL Known
  Issues'' for known limitations.
\item
  Embree CPU support in this release as at Gold level, incorporating the
  same quality and performance as previous releases.
\item
  A small number of API changes were required to get optimal experience
  and performance on the CPU and GPU. See documentation section
  ``Upgrading from Embree 3 to Embree 4'' for details.
\item
  rtcIntersect and rtcOccluded function arguments changed slightly.
\item
  RTCIntersectContext is renamed to RTCRayQuery context and most members
  moved to new RTCIntersectArguments and RTCOccludedArguments
  structures.
\item
  rtcFilterIntersection and rtcFilterOcclusion API calls got replaced by
  rtcInvokeIntersectFilterFromGeometry and
  rtcInvokeOccludedFilterFromGeometry API calls.
\item
  rtcSetGeometryEnableFilterFunctionFromArguments enables argument
  filter functions for some geometry.
\item
  RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_INVOKE\_ARGUMENT\_FILTER ray query flag enables
  argument filter functions for each geometry.
\item
  User geometry callbacks have to return if a valid hit was found.
\item
  Ray masking is enabled by default now as required by most users.
\item
  The default ray mask for geometries got changed from 0xFFFFFFFF to
  0x1.
\item
  Removed ray stream API as rarely used with minimal performance
  benefits over packet tracing.
\item
  Introduced rtcForwardIntersect/rtcForwardOccluded API calls to trace
  tail recursive rays from user geometry callback.
\item
  The rtcGetGeometryUserDataFromScene API call got added to be used in
  SYCL code.
\item
  Added support for user geometry callback function pointer passed
  through ray query context
\item
  Feature flags enable reducing code complexity for optimal performance
  on the GPU.
\item
  Fixed compilation issues for ARM AArch64 processor under Linux.
\item
  Setting default frequency level to SIMD256 for ARM on all platforms.
  This allows using double pumped NEON execution by enabling
  EMBREE\_ISA\_NEON2X in cmake under Linux.
\item
  Fixed missing end caps of motion blurred line segments.
\item
  EMBREE\_ISPC\_SUPPORT is turned OFF by default.
\item
  Embree drops support of the deprecated Intel(R) Compiler. It is
  replaced by the Intel(R) oneAPI DPC++/C++ Compiler on Windows and
  Linux and the Intel(R) C++ Classic Compiler on MacOS (latest tested
  versions is 2023.0.0).
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.13.5}\label{embree-3.13.5}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Fixed bug in bounding flat Catmull Rom curves of subdivision level 4.
\item
  Improved self intersection avoidance for
  RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_DISC\_POINT geometry type. Intersections are
  skipped if the ray origin lies inside the sphere defined by the point
  primitive. Self intersection avoidance can get disabled at compile
  time using the EMBREE\_DISC\_POINT\_SELF\_INTERSECTION\_AVOIDANCE
  cmake option.
\item
  Fixed spatial splitting for non-planar quads.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.13.4}\label{embree-3.13.4}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Using 8-wide BVH and double pumped NEON instructions on Apple M1 gives
  8\% performance boost.
\item
  Fixed binning related crash in SAH BVH builder.
\item
  Added EMBREE\_TBB\_COMPONENT cmake option to define the
  component/library name of Intel® TBB (default: tbb).
\item
  Embree supports now Intel® oneAPI DPC++/C++ Compiler 2022.0.0
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.13.3}\label{embree-3.13.3}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Invalid multi segment motion blurred normal oriented curves are
  properly excluded from BVH build.
\item
  Fixing issue with normal oriented curve construction when center curve
  curvature is very large. Due to this change normal oriented curve
  shape changes slightly.
\item
  Fixed crash caused by disabling a geometry and then detaching it from
  the scene.
\item
  Bugfix in emulated ray packet intersection when EMBREE\_RAY\_PACKETS
  is turned off.
\item
  Bugfix for linear quaternion interpolation fallback.
\item
  Fixed issues with spaces in path to Embree build folder.
\item
  Some fixes to compile Embree in SSE mode using WebAssembly.
\item
  Bugfix for occlusion rays with grids and ray packets.
\item
  We do no longer provide installers for Windows and macOS, please use
  the ZIP files instead.
\item
  Upgrading to Intel® ISPC 1.17.0 for release build.
\item
  Upgrading to Intel® oneTBB 2021.5.0 for release build.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.13.2}\label{embree-3.13.2}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Avoiding spatial split positions that are slightly out of geometry
  bounds.
\item
  Introduced rtcGetGeometryThreadSafe function, which is a thread safe
  version of rtcGetGeometry.
\item
  Using more accurate rcp implementation.
\item
  Bugfix to rare corner case of high quality BVH builder.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.13.1}\label{embree-3.13.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added support for Intel® ISPC ARM target.
\item
  Releases upgrade to Intel® TBB 2021.3.0 and Intel® ISPC 1.16.1
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.13.0}\label{embree-3.13.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added support for Apple M1 CPUs.
\item
  RTC\_SUBDIVISION\_MODE\_NO\_BOUNDARY now works properly for
  non-manifold edges.
\item
  CMake target `uninstall' is not defined if it already exists.
\item
  Embree no longer reads the .embree3 config files, thus all
  configuration has to get passed through the config string to
  rtcNewDevice.
\item
  Releases upgrade to Intel® TBB 2021.2.0 and Intel® ISPC 1.15.0
\item
  Intel® TBB dll is automatically copied into build folder after build
  on windows.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.12.2}\label{embree-3.12.2}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Fixed wrong uv and Ng for grid intersector in robust mode for AVX.
\item
  Removed optimizations for Knights Landing.
\item
  Upgrading release builds to use Intel® oneTBB 2021.1.1
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.12.1}\label{embree-3.12.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Changed default frequency level to SIMD128 for Skylake, Cannon Lake,
  Comet Lake and Tiger Lake CPUs. This change typically improves
  performance for renderers that just use SSE by maintaining higher CPU
  frequencies. In case your renderer is AVX optimized you can get higher
  ray tracing performance by configuring the frequency level to simd256
  through passing frequency\_level=simd256 to rtcNewDevice.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.12.0}\label{embree-3.12.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added linear cone curve geometry support. In this mode a real
  geometric surface for curves with linear basis is rendered using
  capped cones. They are discontinuous at edge boundaries.
\item
  Enabled fast two level builder for instances when low quality build is
  requested.
\item
  Bugfix for BVH build when geometries got disabled.
\item
  Added EMBREE\_BACKFACE\_CULLING\_CURVES cmake option. This allows for
  a cheaper round linear curve intersection when correct internal
  tracking and back hits are not required. The new cmake option defaults
  to OFF.
\item
  User geometries with invalid bounds with lower\textgreater upper in
  some dimension will be ignored.
\item
  Increased robustness for grid interpolation code and fixed returned
  out of range u/v coordinates for grid primitive.
\item
  Fixed handling of motion blur time range for sphere, discs, and
  oriented disc geometries.
\item
  Fixed missing model data in releases.
\item
  Ensure compatibility to newer versions of Intel® oneTBB.
\item
  Motion blur BVH nodes no longer store NaN values.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.11.0}\label{embree-3.11.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Round linear curves now automatically check for the existence of left
  and right connected segments if the flags buffer is empty. Left
  segments exist if the segment(id-1) + 1 == segment(id) and similarly
  for right segments.
\item
  Implemented the min-width feature for curves and points, which allows
  to increase the radius in a distance dependent way, such that the
  curve or points thickness is n pixels wide.
\item
  Round linear curves are closed now also at their start.
\item
  Embree no longer supports Visual Studio 2013 starting with this
  release.
\item
  Bugfix in subdivision tessellation level assignment for non-quad base
  primitives
\item
  Small meshes are directly added to top level build phase of two-level
  builder to reduce memory consumption.
\item
  Enabled fast two level builder for user geometries when low quality
  build is requested.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.10.0}\label{embree-3.10.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added EMBREE\_COMPACT\_POLYS CMake option which enables double indexed
  triangle and quad leaves to reduce memory consumption in compact mode
  by an additional 40\% at about 15\% performance impact. This new mode
  is disabled by default.
\item
  Compile fix for Intel® oneTBB 2021.1-beta05
\item
  Releases upgrade to Intel® TBB 2020.2
\item
  Compile fix for Intel® ISPC v1.13.0
\item
  Adding RPATH to libembree.so in releases
\item
  Increased required CMake version to 3.1.0
\item
  Made instID member for array of pointers ray stream layout optional
  again.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.9.0}\label{embree-3.9.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added round linear curve geometry support. In this mode a real
  geometric surface for curves with linear basis is rendered using
  capped cones with spherical filling between the curve segments.
\item
  Added rtcGetSceneDevice API function, that returns the device a scene
  got created in.
\item
  Improved performance of round curve rendering by up to 1.8x.
\item
  Bugfix to sphere intersection filter invocation for back hit.
\item
  Fixed wrong assertion that triggered for invalid curves which anyway
  get filtered out.
\item
  RelWithDebInfo mode no longer enables assertions.
\item
  Fixed an issue in FindTBB.cmake that caused compile error with Debug
  build under Linux.
\item
  Embree releases no longer provide RPMs for Linux. Please use the RPMs
  coming with the package manager of your Linux distribution.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Embree 3.8.0}\label{embree-3.8.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added collision detection support for user geometries (see rtcCollide
  API function)
\item
  Passing geomID to user geometry callbacks.
\item
  Bugfix in AVX512VL codepath for rtcIntersect1
\item
  For sphere geometries the intersection filter gets now invoked for
  front and back hit.
\item
  Fixed some bugs for quaternion motion blur.
\item
  RTCRayQueryContext always non-const in Embree API
\item
  Made RTCHit aligned to 16 bytes in Embree API
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.7.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.7.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added quaternion motion blur for correct interpolation of rotational
  transformations.
\item
  Fixed wrong bounding calculations when a motion blurred instance did
  instantiate a motion blurred scene.
\item
  In robust mode the depth test consistently uses tnear \textless= t
  \textless= tfar now in order to robustly continue traversal at a
  previous hit point in a way that guarantees reaching all hits, even
  hits at the same place.
\item
  Fixed depth test in robust mode to be precise at tnear and tfar.
\item
  Added next\_hit tutorial to demonstrate robustly collecting all hits
  along a ray using multiple ray queries.
\item
  Implemented robust mode for curves. This has a small performance
  impact but fixes bounding problems with flat curves.
\item
  Improved quality of motion blur BVH by using linear bounds during
  binning.
\item
  Implemented issue with motion blur builder where number of time
  segments for SAH heuristic were counted wrong due to some numerical
  issues.
\item
  Fixed an accuracy issue with rendering very short fat curves.
\item
  rtcCommitScene can now get called during rendering from multiple
  threads to lazily build geometry. When Intel® TBB is used this causes
  a much lower overhead than using rtcJoinCommitScene.
\item
  Geometries can now get attached to multiple scenes at the same time,
  which simplifies mapping general scene graphs to API.
\item
  Updated to Intel® TBB 2019.9 for release builds.
\item
  Fixed a bug in the BVH builder for Grid geometries.
\item
  Added macOS Catalina support to Embree releases.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.6.1}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.6.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Restored binary compatibility between Embree 3.6 and 3.5 when
  single-level instancing is used.
\item
  Fixed bug in subgrid intersector
\item
  Removed point query alignment in Intel® ISPC header
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.6}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.6}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added Catmull-Rom curve types.
\item
  Added support for multi-level instancing.
\item
  Added support for point queries.
\item
  Fixed a bug preventing normal oriented curves being used unless
  timesteps were specified.
\item
  Fixed bug in external BVH builder when configured for dynamic build.
\item
  Added support for new config flag ``user\_threads=N'' to device
  initialization which sets the number of threads used by Intel® TBB but
  created by the user.
\item
  Fixed automatic vertex buffer padding when using rtcSetNewGeometry API
  function.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.5.2}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.5.2}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added EMBREE\_API\_NAMESPACE cmake option that allows to put all
  Embree API functions inside a user defined namespace.
\item
  Added EMBREE\_LIBRARY\_NAME cmake option that allows to rename the
  Embree library.
\item
  When Embree is compiled as static library, EMBREE\_STATIC\_LIB has no
  longer to get defined before including the Embree API headers.
\item
  Added CPU frequency\_level device configuration to allow an
  application to specify the frequency level it wants to run on. This
  forces Embree to not use optimizations that may reduce the CPU
  frequency below that level. By default Embree is configured to the the
  AVX-heavy frequency level, thus if the application uses solely non-AVX
  code, configuring the Embree device with ``frequency\_level=simd128''
  may give better performance.
\item
  Fixed a bug in the spatial split builder which caused it to fail for
  scenes with more than 2\^{}24 geometries.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.5.1}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.5.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Fixed ray/sphere intersector to work also for non-normalized rays.
\item
  Fixed self intersection avoidance for ray oriented discs when
  non-normalized rays were used.
\item
  Increased maximal face valence for subdiv patch to 64 and reduced
  stack size requirement for subdiv patch evaluation.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.5.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.5.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Changed normal oriented curve definition to fix waving artefacts.
\item
  Fixed bounding issue for normal oriented motion blurred curves.
\item
  Fixed performance issue with motion blurred point geometry.
\item
  Fixed generation of documentation with new pandoc versions.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.4.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.4.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added point primitives (spheres, ray-oriented discs, normal-oriented
  discs).
\item
  Fixed crash triggered by scenes with only invalid primitives.
\item
  Improved robustness of quad/grid-based intersectors.
\item
  Upgraded to Intel® TBB 2019.2 for release builds.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.3.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.3.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added support for motion blur time range per geometry. This way
  geometries can appear and disappear during the camera shutter and time
  steps do not have to start and end at camera shutter interval
  boundaries.
\item
  Fixed crash with pathtracer when using --triangle-sphere command line.
\item
  Fixed crash with pathtracer when using --shader ao command line.
\item
  Fixed tutorials showing a black window on macOS 10.14 until moved.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.2.4}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.2.4}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Fixed compile issues with ICC 2019.
\item
  Released ZIP files for Windows are now provided in a version linked
  against Visual Studio 2013 and Visual Studio 2015.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.2.3}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.2.3}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Fixed crash when using curves with RTC\_SCENE\_FLAG\_DYNAMIC combined
  with RTC\_BUILD\_QUALITY\_MEDIUM.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.2.2}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.2.2}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Fixed intersection distance for unnormalized rays with line segments.
\item
  Removed libmmd.dll dependency in release builds for Windows.
\item
  Fixed detection of AppleClang compiler under MacOSX.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.2.1}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.2.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Bugfix in flat mode for hermite curves.
\item
  Added EMBREE\_CURVE\_SELF\_INTERSECTION\_AVOIDANCE\_FACTOR cmake
  option to control self intersection avoidance for flat curves.
\item
  Performance fix when instantiating motion blurred scenes. The
  application should best use two (or more) time steps for an instance
  that instantiates a motion blurred scene.
\item
  Fixed AVX512 compile issue with GCC 6.1.1.
\item
  Fixed performance issue with rtcGetGeometryUserData when used during
  rendering.
\item
  Bugfix in length of derivatives for grid geometry.
\item
  Added BVH8 support for motion blurred curves and lines. For some
  workloads this increases performance by up to 7\%.
\item
  Fixed rtcGetGeometryTransform to return the local to world transform.
\item
  Fixed bug in multi segment motion blur that caused missing of
  perfectly axis aligned geometry.
\item
  Reduced memory consumption of small scenes by 4x.
\item
  Reduced temporal storage of grid builder.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.2.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.2.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Improved watertightness of robust mode.
\item
  Line segments, and other curves are now all contained in a single BVH
  which improves performance when these are both used in a scene.
\item
  Performance improvement of up to 20\% for line segments.
\item
  Bugfix to Embree2 to Embree3 conversion script.
\item
  Added support for Hermite curve basis.
\item
  Semantics of normal buffer for normal oriented curves has changed to
  simplify usage. Please see documentation for details.
\item
  Using GLFW and imgui in tutorials.
\item
  Fixed floating point exception in static variable initialization.
\item
  Fixed invalid memory access in rtcGetGeometryTransform for non-motion
  blur instances.
\item
  Improved self intersection avoidance for flat curves. Transparency
  rays with tnear set to previous hit distance do not need curve radius
  based self intersection avoidance as same hit is calculated again. For
  this reason self intersection avoidance is now only applied to ray
  origin.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.1.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.1.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added new normal-oriented curve primitive for ray tracing of
  grass-like structures.
\item
  Added new grid primitive for ray tracing tessellated and displaced
  surfaces in very memory efficient manner.
\item
  Fixed bug of ribbon curve intersector when derivative was zero.
\item
  Installing all static libraries when EMBREE\_STATIC\_LIB is enabled.
\item
  Added API functions to access topology of subdivision mesh.
\item
  Reduced memory consumption of instances.
\item
  Improved performance of instances by 8\%.
\item
  Reduced memory consumption of curves by up to 2x.
\item
  Up to 5\% higher performance on AVX-512 architectures.
\item
  Added native support for multiple curve basis functions. Internal
  basis conversions are no longer performed, which saves additional
  memory when multiple bases are used.
\item
  Fixed issue with non thread safe local static variable initialization
  in VS2013.
\item
  Bugfix in rtcSetNewGeometry. Vertex buffers did not get properly
  overallocated.
\item
  Replaced ImageMagick with OpenImageIO in the tutorials.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
3.0.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-3.0.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Switched to a new version of the API which provides improved
  flexibility but is not backward compatible. Please see ``Upgrading
  from Embree 2 to Embree 3'' section of the documentation for upgrade
  instructions. In particular, we provide a Python script that performs
  most of the transition work.
\item
  User geometries inside an instanced scene and a top-level scene no
  longer need to handle the instID field of the ray differently. They
  both just need to copy the context.instID into the ray.instID field.
\item
  Support for context filter functions that can be assigned to a ray
  query.
\item
  User geometries can now invoke filter functions using the
  rtcFilterIntersection and rtcFilterOcclusion calls.
\item
  Higher flexibility through specifying build quality per scene and
  geometry.
\item
  Geometry normal uses commonly used right-hand rule from now on.
\item
  Added self-intersection avoidance to ribbon curves and lines.
  Applications do not have to implement self-intersection workarounds
  for these primitive types anymore.
\item
  Added support for 4 billion primitives in a single scene.
\item
  Removed the RTC\_MAX\_USER\_VERTEX\_BUFFERS and
  RTC\_MAX\_INDEX\_BUFFERS limitations.
\item
  Reduced memory consumption by 192 bytes per instance.
\item
  Fixed some performance issues on AVX-512 architectures.
\item
  Individual Contributor License Agreement (ICLA) and Corporate
  Contributor License Agreement (CCLA) no longer required to contribute
  to the project.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.17.5}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.17.5}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Improved watertightness of robust mode.
\item
  Fixed floating point exception in static variable initialization.
\item
  Fixed AVX512 compile issue with GCC 6.1.1.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.17.4}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.17.4}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Fixed AVX512 compile issue with GCC 7.
\item
  Fixed issue with not thread safe local static variable initialization
  in VS2013.
\item
  Fixed bug in the 4 and 8-wide packet intersection of instances with
  multi-segment motion blur on AVX-512 architectures.
\item
  Fixed bug in rtcOccluded4/8/16 when only AVX-512 ISA was enabled.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.17.3}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.17.3}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Fixed GCC compile warning in debug mode.
\item
  Fixed bug of ribbon curve intersector when derivative was zero.
\item
  Installing all static libraries when EMBREE\_STATIC\_LIB is enabled.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.17.2}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.17.2}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Made BVH build of curve geometry deterministic.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.17.1}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.17.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Improved performance of occlusion ray packets by up to 50\%.
\item
  Fixed detection of Clang for CMake 3 under MacOSX
\item
  Fixed AVX code compilation issue with GCC 7 compiler caused by
  explicit use of vzeroupper intrinsics.
\item
  Fixed an issue where Clang address sanitizer reported an error in the
  internal tasking system.
\item
  Added fix to compile on 32 bit Linux distribution.
\item
  Fixed some wrong relative include paths in Embree.
\item
  Improved performance of robust single ray mode by 5\%.
\item
  Added EMBREE\_INSTALL\_DEPENDENCIES option (default OFF) to enable
  installing of Embree dependencies.
\item
  Fixed performance regression for occlusion ray streams.
\item
  Reduced temporary memory requirements of BVH builder for curves and
  line segments.
\item
  Fixed performance regression for user geometries and packet ray
  tracing.
\item
  Fixed bug where wrong closest hit was reported for very curvy hair
  segment.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.17.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.17.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Improved packet ray tracing performance for coherent rays by 10-60\%
  (requires RTC\_INTERSECT\_COHERENT flag).
\item
  Improved ray tracing performance for incoherent rays on AVX-512
  architectures by 5\%.
\item
  Improved ray tracing performance for streams of incoherent rays by
  5-15\%.
\item
  Fixed tbb\_debug.lib linking error under Windows.
\item
  Fast coherent ray stream and packet code paths now also work in robust
  mode.
\item
  Using less aggressive prefetching for large BVH nodes which results in
  1-2\% higher ray tracing performance.
\item
  Precompiled binaries have stack-protector enabled, except for
  traversal kernels. BVH builders can be slightly slower due to this
  change. If you want stack-protectors disabled please turn off
  EMBREE\_STACK\_PROTECTOR in cmake and build the binaries yourself.
\item
  When enabling ISAs individually, the 8-wide BVH was previously only
  available when the AVX ISA was also selected. This issue is now fixed,
  and one can enable only AVX2 and still get best performance by using
  an 8-wide BVH.
\item
  Fixed rtcOccluded1 and rtcOccluded1Ex API functions which were broken
  in Intel® ISPC.
\item
  Providing MSI installer for Windows.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.16.5}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.16.5}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Bugfix in the robust triangle intersector that rarely caused NaNs.
\item
  Fixed bug in hybrid traversal kernel when BVH leaf was entered with no
  active rays. This rarely caused crashes when used with instancing.
\item
  Fixed bug introduced in Embree 2.16.2 which caused instancing not to
  work properly when a smaller than the native SIMD width was used in
  ray packet mode.
\item
  Fixed bug in the curve geometry intersector that caused rendering
  artefacts for Bézier curves with p0=p1 and/or p2=p3.
\item
  Fixed bug in the curve geometry intersector that caused hit results
  with NaNs to be reported.
\item
  Fixed masking bug that caused rare cracks in curve geometry.
\item
  Enabled support for SSE2 in precompiled binaries again.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.16.4}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.16.4}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Bugfix in the ribbon intersector for hair primitives. Non-normalized
  rays caused wrong intersection distance to be reported.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.16.3}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.16.3}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Increased accuracy for handling subdivision surfaces. This fixes
  cracks when using displacement mapping but reduces performance at
  irregular vertices.
\item
  Fixed a bug where subdivision geometry was not properly updated when
  modifying only the tessellation rate and vertex array.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.16.2}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.16.2}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Fixed bug that caused NULL ray query context in intersection filter
  when instancing was used.
\item
  Fixed an issue where uv's where outside the triangle (or quad) for
  very small triangles (or quads). In robust mode we improved the uv
  calculation to avoid that issue, in fast mode we accept that
  inconsistency for better performance.
\item
  Changed UV encoding for non-quad subdivision patches to allow a
  subpatch UV range of \texttt{{[}-0.5,1.5{[}}. Using this new encoding
  one can use finite differences to calculate derivatives if required.
  Please adjust your code in case you rely on the old encoding.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.16.1}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.16.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Workaround for compile issues with Visual Studio 2017
\item
  Fixed bug in subdiv code for static scenes when using tessellation
  levels larger than 50.
\item
  Fixed low performance when adding many geometries to a scene.
\item
  Fixed high memory consumption issue when using instances in dynamic
  scene (by disabling two level builder for user geometries and
  instances).
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.16.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.16.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Improved multi-segment motion blur support for scenes with different
  number of time steps per mesh.
\item
  New top level BVH builder that improves build times and BVH quality of
  two-level BVHs.
\item
  Added support to enable only a single ISA. Previously code was always
  compiled for SSE2.
\item
  Improved single ray tracing performance for incoherent rays on AVX-512
  architectures by 5-10\%.
\item
  Improved packet/hybrid ray tracing performance for incoherent rays on
  AVX-512 architectures by 10-30\%.
\item
  Improved stream ray tracing performance for coherent rays in
  structure-of-pointers layout by 40-70\%.
\item
  BVH builder for compact scenes of triangles and quads needs
  essentially no temporary memory anymore. This doubles the maximal
  scene size that can be rendered in compact mode.
\item
  Triangles no longer store the geometry normal in fast/default mode
  which reduces memory consumption by up to 20\%.
\item
  Compact mode uses BVH4 now consistently which reduces memory
  consumption by up to 10\%.
\item
  Reduced memory consumption for small scenes (of 10k-100k primitives)
  and dynamic scenes.
\item
  Improved performance of user geometries and instances through BVH8
  support.
\item
  The API supports now specifying the geometry ID of a geometry at
  construction time. This way matching the geometry ID used by Embree
  and the application is simplified.
\item
  Fixed a bug that would have caused a failure of the BVH builder for
  dynamic scenes when run on a machine with more then 1000 threads.
\item
  Fixed a bug that could have been triggered when reaching the maximal
  number of mappings under Linux (\texttt{vm.max\_map\_count}). This
  could have happened when creating a large number of small static
  scenes.
\item
  Added huge page support for Windows and MacOSX (experimental).
\item
  Added support for Visual Studio 2017.
\item
  Removed support for Visual Studio 2012.
\item
  Precompiled binaries now require a CPU supporting at least the SSE4.2
  ISA.
\item
  We no longer provide precompiled binaries for 32-bit on Windows.
\item
  Under Windows one now has to use the platform toolset option in CMake
  to switch to Clang or the Intel® Compiler.
\item
  Fixed a bug for subdivision meshes when using the incoherent scene
  flag.
\item
  Fixed a bug in the line geometry intersection, that caused reporting
  an invalid line segment intersection with primID -1.
\item
  Buffer stride for vertex buffers of different time steps of triangle
  and quad meshes have to be identical now.
\item
  Fixed a bug in the curve geometry intersection code when passed a
  perfect cylinder.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.15.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.15.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added \texttt{rtcCommitJoin} mode that allows thread to join a build
  operation. When using the internal tasking system this allows Embree
  to solely use the threads that called \texttt{rtcCommitJoin} to build
  the scene, while previously also normal worker threads participated in
  the build. You should no longer use \texttt{rtcCommit} to join a
  build.
\item
  Added \texttt{rtcDeviceSetErrorFunction2} API call, which sets an
  error callback function which additionally gets passed a user provided
  pointer (\texttt{rtcDeviceSetErrorFunction} is now deprecated).
\item
  Added \texttt{rtcDeviceSetMemoryMonitorFunction2} API call, which sets
  a memory monitor callback function which additionally get passed a
  user provided pointer. (\texttt{rtcDeviceSetMemoryMonitorFunction} is
  now deprecated).
\item
  Build performance for hair geometry improved by up to 2×.
\item
  Standard BVH build performance increased by 5\%.
\item
  Added API extension to use internal Morton-code based builder, the
  standard binned-SAH builder, and the spatial split-based SAH builder.
\item
  Added support for BSpline hair and curves. Embree uses either the
  Bézier or BSpline basis internally, and converts other curves, which
  requires more memory during rendering. For reduced memory consumption
  set the \texttt{EMBREE\_NATIVE\_SPLINE\_BASIS} to the basis your
  application uses (which is set to \texttt{BEZIER} by default).
\item
  Setting the number of threads through
  \texttt{tbb::taskscheduler\_init} object on the application side is
  now working properly.
\item
  Windows and Linux releases are build using AVX-512 support.
\item
  Implemented hybrid traversal for hair and line segments for improved
  ray packet performance.
\item
  AVX-512 code compiles with Clang 4.0.0
\item
  Fixed crash when ray packets were disabled in CMake.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.14.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.14.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added \texttt{ignore\_config\_files} option to init flags that allows
  the application to ignore Embree configuration files.
\item
  Face-varying interpolation is now supported for subdivision surfaces.
\item
  Up to 16 user vertex buffers are supported for vertex attribute
  interpolation.
\item
  Deprecated \texttt{rtcSetBoundaryMode} function, please use the new
  \texttt{rtcSetSubdivisionMode} function.
\item
  Added \texttt{RTC\_SUBDIV\_PIN\_BOUNDARY} mode for handling boundaries
  of subdivision meshes.
\item
  Added \texttt{RTC\_SUBDIV\_PIN\_ALL} mode to enforce linear
  interpolation for subdivision meshes.
\item
  Optimized object generation performance for dynamic scenes.
\item
  Reduced memory consumption when using lots of small dynamic objects.
\item
  Fixed bug for subdivision surfaces using low tessellation rates.
\item
  Hair geometry now uses a new ribbon intersector that intersects with
  ray-facing quads. The new intersector also returns the v-coordinate of
  the hair intersection, and fixes artefacts at junction points between
  segments, at the cost of a small performance hit.
\item
  Added \texttt{rtcSetBuffer2} function, that additionally gets the
  number of elements of a buffer. In dynamic scenes, this function
  allows to quickly change buffer sizes, making it possible to change
  the number of primitives of a mesh or the number of crease features
  for subdivision surfaces.
\item
  Added simple `viewer\_anim' tutorial for rendering key frame
  animations and `buildbench' for measuring BVH (re-)build performance
  for static and dynamic scenes.
\item
  Added more AVX-512 optimizations for future architectures.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.13.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.13.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Improved performance for compact (but not robust) scenes.
\item
  Added robust mode for motion blurred triangles and quads.
\item
  Added fast dynamic mode for user geometries.
\item
  Up to 20\% faster BVH build performance on the second generation
  Intel® Xeon Phi™ processor codenamed Knights Landing.
\item
  Improved quality of the spatial split builder.
\item
  Improved performance for coherent streams of ray packets (SOA layout),
  e.g.~for fast primary visibility.
\item
  Various bug fixes in tessellation cache, quad-based spatial split
  builder, etc.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.12.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.12.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added support for multi-segment motion blur for all primitive types.
\item
  API support for stream of pointers to single rays
  (\texttt{rtcIntersect1Mp} and \texttt{rtcOccluded1Mp})
\item
  Improved BVH refitting performance for dynamic scenes.
\item
  Improved high-quality mode for quads (added spatial split builder for
  quads)
\item
  Faster dynamic scenes for triangle and quad-based meshes on AVX2
  enabled machines.
\item
  Performance and correctness bugfix in optimization for streams of
  coherent (single) rays.
\item
  Fixed large memory consumption (issue introduced in Embree v2.11.0).
  If you use Embree v2.11.0 please upgrade to Embree v2.12.0.
\item
  Reduced memory consumption for dynamic scenes containing small meshes.
\item
  Added support to start and affinitize Intel® TBB worker threads by
  passing ``\texttt{start\_threads=1,set\_affinity=1}'' to
  \texttt{rtcNewDevice}. These settings are recommended on systems with
  a high thread count.
\item
  \texttt{rtcInterpolate2} can now be called within a displacement
  shader.
\item
  Added initial support for Microsoft's Parallel Pattern Library (PPL)
  as tasking system alternative (for optimal performance Intel® TBB is
  highly recommended).
\item
  Updated to Intel® TBB 2017 which is released under the Apache v2.0
  license.
\item
  Dropped support for Visual Studio 2012 Win32 compiler. Visual Studio
  2012 x64 is still supported.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.11.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.11.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Improved performance for streams of coherent (single) rays flagged
  with \texttt{RTC\_INTERSECT\_COHERENT}. For such coherent ray streams,
  e.g. primary rays, the performance typically improves by 1.3-2×.
\item
  New spatial split BVH builder for triangles, which is 2-6× faster than
  the previous version and more memory conservative.
\item
  Improved performance and scalability of all standard BVH builders on
  systems with large core counts.
\item
  Fixed \texttt{rtcGetBounds} for motion blur scenes.
\item
  Thread affinity is now on by default when running on the latest Intel®
  Xeon Phi™ processor.
\item
  Added AVX-512 support for future Intel® Xeon processors.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.10.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.10.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added a new curve geometry which renders the sweep surface of a circle
  along a Bézier curve.
\item
  Intersection filters can update the \texttt{tfar} ray distance.
\item
  Geometry types can get disabled at compile time.
\item
  Modified and extended the ray stream API.
\item
  Added new callback mechanism for the ray stream API.
\item
  Improved ray stream performance (up to 5-10\%).
\item
  Up to 20\% faster morton builder on machines with large core counts.
\item
  Lots of optimizations for the second generation Intel® Xeon Phi™
  processor codenamed Knights Landing.
\item
  Added experimental support for compressed BVH nodes (reduces node size
  to 56-62\% of uncompressed size). Compression introduces a typical
  performance overhead of \textasciitilde10\%.
\item
  Bugfix in backface culling mode. We do now properly cull the backfaces
  and not the frontfaces.
\item
  Feature freeze for the first generation Intel® Xeon Phi™ coprocessor
  codenamed Knights Corner. We will still maintain and add bug fixes to
  Embree v2.9.0, but Embree 2.10 and future versions will no longer
  support it.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.9.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.9.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Improved shadow ray performance (10-100\% depending on the scene).
\item
  Added initial support for ray streams (10-30\% higher performance
  depending on ray coherence in the stream).
\item
  Added support to calculate second order derivatives using the
  \texttt{rtcInterpolate2} function.
\item
  Changed the parametrization for triangular subdivision faces to the
  same scheme used for pentagons.
\item
  Added support to query the Embree configuration using the
  \texttt{rtcDeviceGetParameter} function.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.8.1}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.8.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added support for setting per geometry tessellation rate (supported
  for subdivision and Bézier geometries).
\item
  Added support for motion blurred instances.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.8.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.8.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added support for line segment geometry.
\item
  Added support for quad geometry (replaces triangle-pairs feature).
\item
  Added support for linear motion blur of user geometries.
\item
  Improved performance through AVX-512 optimizations.
\item
  Improved performance of lazy scene build (when using Intel® TBB 4.4
  update 2).
\item
  Improved performance through huge page support under linux.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.7.1}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.7.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Internal tasking system supports cancellation of build operations.
\item
  Intel® ISPC mode for robust and compact scenes got significantly
  faster (implemented hybrid traversal for bvh4.triangle4v and
  bvh4.triangle4i).
\item
  Hair rendering got faster as we fixed some issues with the SAH
  heuristic cost factors.
\item
  BVH8 got slight faster for single ray traversal (improved sorting when
  hitting more than 4 boxes).
\item
  BVH build performance got up to 30\% faster on CPUs with high core
  counts (improved parallel partition code).
\item
  High quality build mode again working properly (spatial splits had
  been deactivated in v2.7.0 due to some bug).
\item
  Support for merging two adjacent triangles sharing a common edge into
  a triangle-pair primitive (can reduce memory consumption and BVH build
  times by up to 50\% for mostly quad-based input meshes).
\item
  Internal cleanups (reduced number of traversal kernels by more
  templating).
\item
  Reduced stack size requirements of BVH builders.
\item
  Fixed crash for dynamic scenes, triggered by deleting all geometries
  from the scene.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.7.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.7.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added device concept to Embree to allow different components of an
  application to use Embree without interfering with each other.
\item
  Fixed memory leak in twolevel builder used for dynamic scenes.
\item
  Fixed bug in tessellation cache that caused crashes for subdivision
  surfaces.
\item
  Fixed bug in internal task scheduler that caused deadlocks when using
  \texttt{rtcCommitThread}.
\item
  Improved hit-distance accuracy for thin triangles in robust mode.
\item
  Added support to disable ray packet support in cmake.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.6.2}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.6.2}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Fixed bug triggered by instantiating motion blur geometry.
\item
  Fixed bug in hit UV coordinates of static subdivision geometries.
\item
  Performance improvements when only changing tessellation levels for
  subdivision geometry per frame.
\item
  Added ray packet intersectors for subdivision geometry, resulting in
  improved performance for coherent rays.
\item
  Reduced virtual address space usage for static geometries.
\item
  Fixed some AVX2 code paths when compiling with GCC or Clang.
\item
  Bugfix for subdiv patches with non-matching winding order.
\item
  Bugfix in ISA detection of AVX-512.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.6.1}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.6.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Major performance improvements for ray tracing subdivision surfaces,
  e.g.~up to 2× faster for scenes where only the tessellation levels are
  changing per frame, and up to 3× faster for scenes with lots of crease
  features
\item
  Initial support for architectures supporting the new 16-wide AVX-512
  ISA
\item
  Implemented intersection filter callback support for subdivision
  surfaces
\item
  Added \texttt{RTC\_IGNORE\_INVALID\_RAYS} CMake option which makes the
  ray intersectors more robust against full tree traversal caused by
  invalid ray inputs (e.g.~INF, NaN, etc)
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.6.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.6.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Added \texttt{rtcInterpolate} function to interpolate per vertex
  attributes
\item
  Added \texttt{rtcSetBoundaryMode} function that can be used to select
  the boundary handling for subdivision surfaces
\item
  Fixed a traversal bug that caused rays with very small ray direction
  components to miss geometry
\item
  Performance improvements for the robust traversal mode
\item
  Fixed deadlock when calling \texttt{rtcCommit} from multiple threads
  on same scene
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.5.1}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.5.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  On dual socket workstations, the initial BVH build performance almost
  doubled through a better memory allocation scheme
\item
  Reduced memory usage for subdivision surface objects with crease
  features
\item
  \texttt{rtcCommit} performance is robust against unset ``flush to
  zero'' and ``denormals are zero'' flags. However, enabling these flags
  in your application is still recommended
\item
  Reduced memory usage for subdivision surfaces with borders and
  infinitely sharp creases
\item
  Lots of internal cleanups and bug fixes for both Intel® Xeon® and
  Intel® Xeon Phi™
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.5.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.5.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Improved hierarchy build performance on both Intel Xeon and Intel Xeon
  Phi
\item
  Vastly improved tessellation cache for ray tracing subdivision
  surfaces
\item
  Added \texttt{rtcGetUserData} API call to query per geometry user
  pointer set through \texttt{rtcSetUserData}
\item
  Added support for memory monitor callback functions to track and limit
  memory consumption
\item
  Added support for progress monitor callback functions to track build
  progress and cancel long build operations
\item
  BVH builders can be used to build user defined hierarchies inside the
  application (see tutorial \hyperref[bvh-builder]{BVH Builder})
\item
  Switched to Intel® TBB as default tasking system on Xeon to get even
  faster hierarchy build times and better integration for applications
  that also use Intel® TBB
\item
  \texttt{rtcCommit} can get called from multiple Intel® TBB threads to
  join the hierarchy build operations
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.4}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.4}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Support for Catmull Clark subdivision surfaces (triangle/quad base
  primitives)
\item
  Support for vector displacements on Catmull Clark subdivision surfaces
\item
  Various bug fixes (e.g.~4-byte alignment of vertex buffers works)
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.3.3}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.3.3}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  BVH builders more robustly handle invalid input data (Intel Xeon
  processor family)
\item
  Motion blur support for hair geometry (Xeon)
\item
  Improved motion blur performance for triangle geometry (Xeon)
\item
  Improved robust ray tracing mode (Xeon)
\item
  Added \texttt{rtcCommitThread} API call for easier integration into
  existing tasking systems (Xeon and Intel Xeon Phi coprocessor)
\item
  Added support for recording and replaying all
  \texttt{rtcIntersect}/\texttt{rtcOccluded} calls (Xeon and Xeon Phi)
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.3.2}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.3.2}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Improved mixed AABB/OBB-BVH for hair geometry (Xeon Phi)
\item
  Reduced amount of pre-allocated memory for BVH builders (Xeon Phi)
\item
  New 64-bit Morton code-based BVH builder (Xeon Phi)
\item
  (Enhanced) Morton code-based BVH builders use now tree rotations to
  improve BVH quality (Xeon Phi)
\item
  Bug fixes (Xeon and Xeon Phi)
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.3.1}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.3.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  High quality BVH mode improves spatial splits which result in up to
  30\% performance improvement for some scenes (Xeon)
\item
  Compile time enabled intersection filter functions do not reduce
  performance if no intersection filter is used in the scene (Xeon and
  Xeon Phi)
\item
  Improved ray tracing performance for hair geometry by \textgreater20\%
  on Xeon Phi. BVH for hair geometry requires 20\% less memory
\item
  BVH8 for AVX/AVX2 targets improves performance for single ray tracing
  on Haswell by up to 12\% and by up to 5\% for hybrid (Xeon)
\item
  Memory conservative BVH for Xeon Phi now uses BVH node quantization to
  lower memory footprint (requires half the memory footprint of the
  default BVH)
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.3}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.3}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Support for ray tracing hair geometry (Xeon and Xeon Phi)
\item
  Catching errors through error callback function
\item
  Faster hybrid traversal (Xeon and Xeon Phi)
\item
  New memory conservative BVH for Xeon Phi
\item
  Faster Morton code-based builder on Xeon
\item
  Faster binned-SAH builder on Xeon Phi
\item
  Lots of code cleanups/simplifications/improvements (Xeon and Xeon Phi)
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.2}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.2}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Support for motion blur on Xeon Phi
\item
  Support for intersection filter callback functions
\item
  Support for buffer sharing with the application
\item
  Lots of AVX2 optimizations, e.g.~\textasciitilde20\% faster 8-wide
  hybrid traversal
\item
  Experimental support for 8-wide (AVX/AVX2) and 16-wide BVHs (Xeon Phi)
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.1}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  New future proof API with a strong focus on supporting dynamic scenes
\item
  Lots of optimizations for 8-wide AVX2 (Haswell architecture)
\item
  Automatic runtime code selection for SSE, AVX, and AVX2
\item
  Support for user-defined geometry
\item
  New and improved BVH builders:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Fast adaptive Morton code-based builder (without SAH-based top-level
    rebuild)
  \item
    Both the SAH and Morton code-based builders got faster (Xeon Phi)
  \item
    New variant of the SAH-based builder using triangle pre-splits (Xeon
    Phi)
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\subsection{New Features in Embree
2.0}\label{new-features-in-embree-2.0}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Support for the Intel® Xeon Phi™ coprocessor platform
\item
  Support for high-performance ``packet'' kernels on SSE, AVX, and Xeon
  Phi
\item
  Integration with the Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler (Intel®
  ISPC)
\item
  Instantiation and fast BVH reconstruction
\item
  Example photo-realistic rendering engine for both C++ and Intel® ISPC
\end{itemize}

\chapter{Installation of Embree}\label{installation-of-embree}

\section{Windows Installation}\label{windows-installation}

Embree linked against Visual Studio 2015 are provided as a ZIP file
\href{https://github.com/embree/embree/releases/download/v4.1.0/embree-4.1.0.x64.vc14.windows.zip}{embree-4.1.0.x64.vc14.windows.zip}.
After unpacking this ZIP file, you should set the path to the
\texttt{lib} folder manually to your \texttt{PATH} environment variable
for applications to find Embree.

\section{Linux Installation}\label{linux-installation}

The Linux version of Embree is also delivered as a \texttt{tar.gz} file:
\href{https://github.com/embree/embree/releases/download/v4.1.0/embree-4.1.0.x86_64.linux.tar.gz}{embree-4.1.0.x86\_64.linux.tar.gz}.
Unpack this file using \texttt{tar} and source the provided
\texttt{embree-vars.sh} (if you are using the bash shell) or
\texttt{embree-vars.csh} (if you are using the C shell) to set up the
environment properly:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{tar xzf embree}\OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{4.1}\ErrorTok{.0.x86\_64.linux.tar.gz}
\NormalTok{source embree}\OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{4.1}\ErrorTok{.0.x86\_64.linux}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{embree}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{vars}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{sh}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

We recommend adding a relative \texttt{RPATH} to your application that
points to the location where Embree (and TBB) can be found,
e.g.~\texttt{\$ORIGIN/../lib}.

\section{macOS Installation}\label{macos-installation}

The macOS version of Embree is also delivered as a ZIP file:
\href{https://github.com/embree/embree/releases/download/v4.1.0/embree-4.1.0.x86_64.macosx.zip}{embree-4.1.0.x86\_64.macosx.zip}.
Unpack this file using \texttt{tar} and source the provided
\texttt{embree-vars.sh} (if you are using the bash shell) or
\texttt{embree-vars.csh} (if you are using the C shell) to set up the
environment properly:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{unzip embree}\OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{4.1}\ErrorTok{.0.x64.macosx.zip}\NormalTok{    source embree}\OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{4.1}\ErrorTok{.0.x64.macosx}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{embree}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{vars}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{sh}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

If you want to ship Embree with your application, please use the Embree
library of the provided ZIP file. The library name of that Embree
library is of the form \texttt{@rpath/libembree.4.dylib} (and similar
also for the included TBB library). This ensures that you can add a
relative \texttt{RPATH} to your application that points to the location
where Embree (and TBB) can be found, e.g.~\texttt{@loader\_path/../lib}.

\section{Building Embree
Applications}\label{building-embree-applications}

The most convenient way to build an Embree application is through CMake.
Just let CMake find your unpacked Embree package using the
\texttt{FIND\_PACKAGE} function inside your \texttt{CMakeLists.txt}
file:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{ FIND\_PACKAGE}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{embree }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{ REQUIRED}\OperatorTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

For CMake to properly find Embree you need to set the
\texttt{embree\_DIR} variable to the folder containing the
\texttt{embree\_config.cmake} file. You might also have to set the
\texttt{TBB\_DIR} variable to the path containing
\texttt{TBB-config.cmake} of a local TBB install, in case you do not
have TBB installed globally on your system, e.g:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{cmake }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{D embree\_DIR}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{path\_to\_embree\_package}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{lib}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{cmake}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{embree}\OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{4.1}\ErrorTok{.0}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ \textbackslash{}}
      \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{D TBB\_DIR}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{path\_to\_tbb\_package}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{lib}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{cmake}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{tbb}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ \textbackslash{}}
      \OperatorTok{..}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The \texttt{FIND\_PACKAGE} function will create an \texttt{embree}
target that you can add to your target link libraries:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{TARGET\_LINK\_LIBRARIES}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{application embree}\OperatorTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

For a full example on how to build an Embree application please have a
look at the \texttt{minimal} tutorial provided in the \texttt{src}
folder of the Embree package and also the contained \texttt{README.txt}
file.

\section{Building Embree SYCL
Applications}\label{building-embree-sycl-applications}

Building Embree SYCL applications is also best done using CMake. Please
first get some compatible SYCL compiler and setup the environment as
decribed in sections \hyperref[linux-sycl-compilation]{Linux SYCL
Compilation} and \hyperref[windows-sycl-compilation]{Windows SYCL
Compilation}.

Also perform the setup steps from the previous
\hyperref[building-embree-applications]{Building Embree Applications}
section.

Please also have a look at the \hyperref[minimal]{Minimal} tutorial that
is provided with the Embree release, for an example how to build a
simple SYCL application using CMake and Embree.

To properly compile your SYCL application you have to add additional
SYCL compile flags for each C++ file that contains SYCL device side code
or kernels as described next.

\subsection{JIT Compilation}\label{jit-compilation}

We recommend using just in time compilation (JIT compilation) together
with \hyperref[sycl-jit-caching]{SYCL JIT caching} to compile Embree
SYCL applications. For JIT compilation add these options to the
compilation phase of all C++ files that contain SYCL code:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{fsycl }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{Xclang }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{fsycl}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{allow}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{func}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ptr }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{fsycl}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{targets}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spir64}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

These options enable SYCL two phase compilation (\texttt{-fsycl}
option), enable function pointer support
(\texttt{-Xclang\ -fsycl-allow-func-ptr} option), and just in time (JIT)
compilation only (\texttt{-fsycl-targets=spir64} option).

The following link options have to get added to the linking stage of
your application when using just in time compilation:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{fsycl }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{fsycl}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{targets}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spir64}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

For a full example on how to build an Embree SYCL application please
have a look at the SYCL version of the \texttt{minimal} tutorial
provided in the \texttt{src} folder of the Embree package and also the
contained \texttt{README.txt} file.

Please have a look at the \hyperref[compiling-embree]{Compiling Embree}
section on how to create an Embree package from sources if required.

\subsection{AOT Compilation}\label{aot-compilation}

Ahead of time compilation (AOT compilation) allows to speed up first
application start up time as device binaries are precompiled. We do not
recommend using AOT compilation as it does not allow the usage of
specialization constants to reduce code complexity.

For ahead of time compilation add these compile options to the
compilation phase of all C++ files that contain SYCL code:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{fsycl }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{Xclang }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{fsycl}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{allow}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{func}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ptr }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{fsycl}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{targets}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spir64\_gen}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

These options enable SYCL two phase compilation (\texttt{-fsycl}
option), enable function pointer support
(\texttt{-Xclang\ -fsycl-allow-func-ptr} option), and ahead of time
(AOT) compilation (\texttt{-fsycl-targets=spir64\_gen} option).

The following link options have to get added to the linking stage of
your application when compiling ahead of time for Xe HPG devices:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{fsycl }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{fsycl}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{targets}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spir64\_gen}
\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{Xsycl}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{target}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{backend}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spir64\_gen }\StringTok{"{-}device XE\_HPG\_CORE"}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This in particular configures the devices for AOT compilation to
\texttt{XE\_HPG\_CORE}.

To get a list of all device supported by AOT compilation look at the
help of the device option in ocloc tool:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{ocloc compile }\OperatorTok{{-}{-}}\NormalTok{help}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\chapter{Compiling Embree}\label{compiling-embree}

We recommend using the prebuild Embree packages from
\url{https://github.com/embree/embree/releases}. If you need to compile
Embree yourself you need to use CMake as described in the following.

Do not enable fast-math optimizations in your compiler as this mode is
not supported by Embree.

\section{Linux and macOS}\label{linux-and-macos}

To compile Embree you need a modern C++ compiler that supports C++11.
Embree is tested with the following compilers:

Linux

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Intel® oneAPI DPC++/C++ Compiler 2023.1.0
\item
  Intel® oneAPI DPC++/C++ Compiler 2023.0.0
\item
  oneAPI DPC++/C++ Compiler 2023-04-17
\item
  Clang 5.0.0
\item
  Clang 4.0.0
\item
  GCC 10.0.1 (Fedora 32) AVX512 support
\item
  GCC 8.3.1 (Fedora 28) AVX512 support
\item
  GCC 7.3.1 (Fedora 27) AVX2 support
\item
  GCC 7.3.1 (Fedora 26) AVX2 support
\item
  GCC 6.4.1 (Fedora 25) AVX2 support
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.19.0
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.18.1
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.17.0
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.16.1
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.15.0
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.14.1
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.13.0
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.12.0
\end{itemize}

macOS x86

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Intel® C++ Classic Compiler 2023.1.0
\item
  Intel® C++ Classic Compiler 2023.0.0
\item
  Apple Clang 12.0.5 (macOS 11.7.1)
\end{itemize}

macOS M1

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Apple Clang 12.0.5 (macOS 11.7.1)
\end{itemize}

Embree supports using the Intel® Threading Building Blocks (TBB) as the
tasking system. For performance and flexibility reasons we recommend
using Embree with the Intel® Threading Building Blocks (TBB) and best
also use TBB inside your application. Optionally you can disable TBB in
Embree through the \texttt{EMBREE\_TASKING\_SYSTEM} CMake variable.

Embree supports the Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler (Intel® ISPC),
which allows straightforward parallelization of an entire renderer. If
you want to use Intel® ISPC then you can enable
\texttt{EMBREE\_ISPC\_SUPPORT} in CMake. Download and install the Intel®
ISPC binaries from
\href{https://ispc.github.io/downloads.html}{ispc.github.io}. After
installation, put the path to \texttt{ispc} permanently into your
\texttt{PATH} environment variable or you set the
\texttt{EMBREE\_ISPC\_EXECUTABLE} variable to point at the ISPC
executable during CMake configuration.

You additionally have to install CMake 3.1.0 or higher and the developer
version of \href{https://www.glfw.org/}{GLFW} version 3.

Under macOS, all these dependencies can be installed using
\href{http://www.macports.org/}{MacPorts}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{sudo port install cmake tbb}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{devel glfw}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{devel}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Depending on your Linux distribution you can install these dependencies
using \texttt{yum} or \texttt{apt-get}. Some of these packages might
already be installed or might have slightly different names.

Type the following to install the dependencies using \texttt{yum}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{sudo yum install cmake}
\NormalTok{sudo yum install tbb}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{devel}
\NormalTok{sudo yum install glfw}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{devel}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Type the following to install the dependencies using \texttt{apt-get}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{sudo apt}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{get install cmake}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{curses}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{gui}
\NormalTok{sudo apt}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{get install libtbb}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{dev}
\NormalTok{sudo apt}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{get install libglfw3}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{dev}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Finally, you can compile Embree using CMake. Create a build directory
inside the Embree root directory and execute \texttt{ccmake\ ..} inside
this build directory.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{mkdir build}
\NormalTok{cd build}
\NormalTok{ccmake }\OperatorTok{..}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Per default, CMake will use the compilers specified with the \texttt{CC}
and \texttt{CXX} environment variables. Should you want to use a
different compiler, run \texttt{cmake} first and set the
\texttt{CMAKE\_CXX\_COMPILER} and \texttt{CMAKE\_C\_COMPILER} variables
to the desired compiler. For example, to use the Clang compiler instead
of the default GCC on most Linux machines (\texttt{g++} and
\texttt{gcc}), execute

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{cmake }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{DCMAKE\_CXX\_COMPILER}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{clang}\OperatorTok{++} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{DCMAKE\_C\_COMPILER}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{clang }\OperatorTok{..}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Running \texttt{ccmake} will open a dialog where you can perform various
configurations as described below in
\hyperref[cmake-configuration]{CMake Configuration}. After having
configured Embree, press \texttt{c} (for configure) and \texttt{g} (for
generate) to generate a Makefile and leave the configuration. The code
can be compiled by executing make.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{make }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{j }\DecValTok{8}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The executables will be generated inside the build folder. We recommend
installing the Embree library and header files on your system. Therefore
set the \texttt{CMAKE\_INSTALL\_PREFIX} to \texttt{/usr} in cmake and
type:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{sudo make install}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

If you keep the default \texttt{CMAKE\_INSTALL\_PREFIX} of
\texttt{/usr/local} then you have to make sure the path
\texttt{/usr/local/lib} is in your \texttt{LD\_LIBRARY\_PATH}.

You can also uninstall Embree again by executing:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{sudo make uninstall}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

You can also create an Embree package using the following command:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{make package}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Please see the \hyperref[building-embree-applications]{Building Embree
Applications} section on how to build your application with such an
Embree package.

\section{Linux SYCL Compilation}\label{linux-sycl-compilation}

There are two options to compile Embree with SYCL support: The open
source \href{https://github.com/intel/llvm/}{``oneAPI DPC++ Compiler''}
or the
\href{https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/developer/articles/tool/oneapi-standalone-components.html\#dpcpp-cpp}{``Intel(R)
oneAPI DPC++/C++ Compiler''}. Other SYCL compilers are not supported.

The ``oneAPI DPC++ Compiler'' is more up-to-date than the ``Intel(R)
oneAPI DPC++/C++ Compiler'' but less stable. The current tested version
of the ``oneAPI DPC++ compiler is

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \href{https://github.com/intel/llvm/releases/download/sycl-nightly\%2F20230417/dpcpp-compiler.tar.gz}{oneAPI
  DPC++ Compiler 2023-04-17}
\end{itemize}

The compiler can be downloaded and simply extracted. The oneAPI DPC++
compiler 2022-12-14 can be set up executing the following command in a
Linux (bash) shell:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{wget https}\OperatorTok{:}\CommentTok{//github.com/intel/llvm/releases/download/sycl{-}nightly\%2F20230417/dpcpp{-}compiler.tar.gz}
\NormalTok{tar xzf dpcpp}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{compiler}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{tar}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{gz}
\NormalTok{source }\OperatorTok{./}\NormalTok{dpcpp\_compiler}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{startup}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{sh}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The \texttt{startup.sh} script will put \texttt{clang++} and
\texttt{clang} from the oneAPI DPC++ Compiler into your path.

Please also install all Linux packages described in the previous
section.

Now, you can configure Embree using CMake by executing the following
command in the Embree root directory:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{cmake }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{B build \textbackslash{}}
      \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{DCMAKE\_CXX\_COMPILER}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{clang}\OperatorTok{++}\NormalTok{ \textbackslash{}}
      \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{DCMAKE\_C\_COMPILER}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{clang \textbackslash{}}
      \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{DEMBREE\_SYCL\_SUPPORT}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ON}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This will create a directory \texttt{build} to use as the CMake build
directory, configure the usage of the oneAPI DPC++ Compiler, and turn on
SYCL support through \texttt{EMBREE\_SYCL\_SUPPORT=ON}.

Alternatively, you can download and run the installer of the

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \href{https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/developer/articles/tool/oneapi-standalone-components.html\#dpcpp-cpp}{Intel(R)
  oneAPI DPC++/C++ Compiler 2023.1.0}.
\end{itemize}

After installation, you can set up the compiler by sourcing the
\texttt{vars.sh} script in the \texttt{env} directory of the compiler
install directory, for example,

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{source }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{opt}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{intel}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{oneAPI}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{compiler}\OperatorTok{/}\FloatTok{2023.0}\ErrorTok{.0}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{env}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{vars}\OperatorTok{.}\NormalTok{sh}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This script will put the \texttt{icpx} and \texttt{icx} compiler
executables from the Intel(R) oneAPI DPC++/C++ Compiler in your path.

Now, you can configure Embree using CMake by executing the following
command in the Embree root directory:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{cmake }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{B build \textbackslash{}}
      \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{DCMAKE\_CXX\_COMPILER}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{icpx \textbackslash{}}
      \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{DCMAKE\_C\_COMPILER}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{icx \textbackslash{}}
      \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{DEMBREE\_SYCL\_SUPPORT}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ON}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

More information about setting up the Intel(R) oneAPI DPC++/C++ compiler
can be found in the
\href{https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/develop/documentation/oneapi-dpcpp-cpp-compiler-dev-guide-and-reference/top/compiler-setup.html}{Development
Reference Guide}. Please note, that the Intel(R) oneAPI DPC++/C++
compiler requires
\href{https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/develop/documentation/oneapi-dpcpp-cpp-compiler-dev-guide-and-reference/top/compiler-setup/use-the-command-line/use-cmake-with-the-compiler.html}{at
least CMake version 3.20.5 on Linux}.

Independent of the DPC++ compiler choice, you can now build Embree using

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{cmake }\OperatorTok{{-}{-}}\NormalTok{build build }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{j }\DecValTok{8}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The executables will be generated inside the build folder. The
executable names of the SYCL versions of the tutorials end with
\texttt{\_sycl}.

\subsection{Linux Graphics Driver
Installation}\label{linux-graphics-driver-installation}

To run the SYCL code you need to install the latest GPGPU drivers for
your Intel Xe HPG/HPC GPUs from here \url{https://dgpu-docs.intel.com/}.
Follow the driver installation instructions for your graphics card and
operating system.

We tested Embree with the latest GPGPU driver Devel Release from
20220809. The Intel(R) Graphics Compute Runtime for oneAPI Level Zero
and OpenCL(TM) Driver from that release is too old for Embree to work
properly. Thus if no newer version of the GPGPU driver is available, you
need to additionally install the latest compute runtime from here
\href{https://github.com/intel/compute-runtime/releases/tag/23.09.25812.14}{23.09.25812.14}.

Unfortunately, these compute runtime packages are only available for
Ubuntu 22.04. You can also install a newer version of the compute
runtime if available.

\section{Windows}\label{windows}

Embree is tested using the following compilers under Windows:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Intel® oneAPI DPC++/C++ Compiler 2023.1.0
\item
  Intel® oneAPI DPC++/C++ Compiler 2023.0.0
\item
  oneAPI DPC++/C++ Compiler 2023-04-17
\item
  Visual Studio 2019
\item
  Visual Studio 2017
\item
  Visual Studio 2015 (Update~1)
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.19.0
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.18.1
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.17.0
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.16.1
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.15.0
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.14.1
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.13.0
\item
  Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler 1.12.0
\end{itemize}

To compile Embree for AVX-512 you have to use the Intel® Compiler.

Embree supports using the Intel® Threading Building Blocks (TBB) as the
tasking system. For performance and flexibility reasons we recommend
using use Embree with the Intel® Threading Building Blocks (TBB) and
best also use TBB inside your application. Optionally you can disable
TBB in Embree through the \texttt{EMBREE\_TASKING\_SYSTEM} CMake
variable.

Embree will either find the Intel® Threading Building Blocks (TBB)
installation that comes with the Intel® Compiler, or you can install the
binary distribution of TBB directly from
\url{https://github.com/oneapi-src/oneTBB/releases} into a folder named
\texttt{tbb} into your Embree root directory. You also have to make sure
that the libraries \texttt{tbb.dll} and \texttt{tbb\_malloc.dll} can be
found when executing your Embree applications, e.g.~by putting the path
to these libraries into your \texttt{PATH} environment variable.

Embree supports the Intel® Implicit SPMD Program Compiler (Intel® ISPC),
