\NormalTok{rtcNewGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{device}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_SPHERE\_POINT}\OperatorTok{);}
\NormalTok{rtcNewGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{device}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_DISC\_POINT}\OperatorTok{);}
\NormalTok{rtcNewGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{device}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_ORIENTED\_DISC\_POINT}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

Points with per vertex radii are supported with sphere, ray-oriented
discs, and normal-oriented discs geometric representations. Such point
geometries are created by passing
\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_SPHERE\_POINT},
\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_DISC\_POINT}, or
\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_ORIENTED\_DISC\_POINT} to the
\texttt{rtcNewGeometry} function. The point vertices can be specified t
through a vertex buffer (\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX}). For the
normal oriented discs a normal buffer
(\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_NORMAL}) has to get specified additionally.
See \texttt{rtcSetGeometryBuffer} and
\texttt{rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer} for more details on how to set
buffers.

The vertex buffer stores each control vertex in the form of a single
precision position and radius stored in (\texttt{x}, \texttt{y},
\texttt{z}, \texttt{r}) order in memory (\texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT4}
format). The number of vertices is inferred from the size of this
buffer. Similarly, the normal buffer stores a single precision normal
per control vertex (\texttt{x}, \texttt{y}, \texttt{z} order and
\texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT3} format).

In the \texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_SPHERE\_POINT} mode, a real
geometric surface is rendered for the curve, which is more expensive but
allows closeup views.

The \texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_DISC\_POINT} flat mode is a fast mode
designed to render distant points. In this mode the point is rendered as
a ray facing disc.

The \texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_ORIENTED\_DISC\_POINT} mode is a mode
designed as a midpoint geometrically between ray facing discs and
spheres. In this mode the point is rendered as a normal oriented disc.

For all point types, only the hit distance and geometry normal is
returned as hit information, u and v are set to zero.

For multi-segment motion blur, the number of time steps must be first
specified using the \texttt{rtcSetGeometryTimeStepCount} call. Then a
vertex buffer for each time step can be set using different buffer
slots, and all these buffers must have the same stride and size.

Also see tutorial {[}Points{]} for an example of how to create and use
point geometries.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure \texttt{NULL} is returned and an error code is set that can
be queried using \texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewgeometry]{rtcNewGeometry}

\pagebreak

\section{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_USER}\label{rtc_geometry_type_user}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_USER }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ user geometry type}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\NormalTok{RTCGeometry geometry }\OperatorTok{=}
\NormalTok{  rtcNewGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{device}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_USER}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

User-defined geometries contain a number of user-defined primitives,
just like triangle meshes contain multiple triangles. The shape of the
user-defined primitives is specified through registered callback
functions, which enable extending Embree with arbitrary types of
primitives.

User-defined geometries are created by passing
\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_USER} to the \texttt{rtcNewGeometry}
function call. One has to set the number of primitives (see
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryUserPrimitiveCount}), a user data pointer (see
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryUserData}), a bounding function closure (see
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryBoundsFunction}), as well as user-defined
intersect (see \texttt{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFunction}) and occluded
(see \texttt{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFunction}) callback functions. The
bounding function is used to query the bounds of all time steps of a
user primitive, while the intersect and occluded callback functions are
called to intersect the primitive with a ray. The user data pointer is
passed to each callback invocation and can be used to point to the
application's representation of the user geometry.

The creation of a user geometry typically looks the following:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTCGeometry geometry }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rtcNewGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{device}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_USER}\OperatorTok{);}
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryUserPrimitiveCount}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{geometry}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ numPrimitives}\OperatorTok{);}
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryUserData}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{geometry}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ userGeometryRepresentation}\OperatorTok{);}
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryBoundsFunction}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{geometry}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ boundsFunction}\OperatorTok{);}
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFunction}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{geometry}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ intersectFunction}\OperatorTok{);}
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFunction}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{geometry}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ occludedFunction}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Please have a look at the \texttt{rtcSetGeometryBoundsFunction},
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFunction}, and
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFunction} functions on the implementation
of the callback functions.

Primitives of a user geometry are ignored during rendering when their
bounds are empty, thus bounds have lower\textgreater upper in at least
one dimension.

See tutorial \hyperref[user-geometry]{User Geometry} for an example of
how to use the user-defined geometries.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure \texttt{NULL} is returned and an error code is set that can
be queried using \texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewgeometry]{rtcNewGeometry},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryuserprimitivecount]{rtcSetGeometryUserPrimitiveCount},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryuserdata]{rtcSetGeometryUserData},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryboundsfunction]{rtcSetGeometryBoundsFunction},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryintersectfunction]{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFunction},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryoccludedfunction]{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFunction}

\pagebreak

\section{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_INSTANCE}\label{rtc_geometry_type_instance}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_INSTANCE }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ instance geometry type}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\NormalTok{RTCGeometry geometry }\OperatorTok{=}
\NormalTok{   rtcNewGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{device}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_INSTANCE}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

Embree supports instancing of scenes using affine transformations (3×3
matrix plus translation). As the instanced scene is stored only a single
time, even if instanced to multiple locations, this feature can be used
to create very complex scenes with small memory footprint.

Embree supports both single-level instancing and multi-level instancing.
The maximum instance nesting depth is
\texttt{RTC\_MAX\_INSTANCE\_LEVEL\_COUNT}; it can be configured at
compile-time using the constant
\texttt{EMBREE\_MAX\_INSTANCE\_LEVEL\_COUNT}. Users should adapt this
constant to their needs: instances nested any deeper are silently
ignored in release mode, and cause assertions in debug mode.

Instances are created by passing \texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_INSTANCE}
to the \texttt{rtcNewGeometry} function call. The instanced scene can be
set using the \texttt{rtcSetGeometryInstancedScene} call, and the affine
transformation can be set using the \texttt{rtcSetGeometryTransform}
function.

Please note that \texttt{rtcCommitScene} on the instanced scene should
be called first, followed by \texttt{rtcCommitGeometry} on the instance,
followed by \texttt{rtcCommitScene} for the top-level scene containing
the instance.

If a ray hits the instance, the \texttt{geomID} and \texttt{primID}
members of the hit are set to the geometry ID and primitive ID of the
hit primitive in the instanced scene, and the \texttt{instID} member of
the hit is set to the geometry ID of the instance in the top-level
scene.

The instancing scheme can also be implemented using user geometries. To
achieve this, the user geometry code should set the \texttt{instID}
member of the ray query context to the geometry ID of the instance, then
trace the transformed ray, and finally set the \texttt{instID} field of
the ray query context again to -1. The \texttt{instID} field is copied
automatically by each primitive intersector into the \texttt{instID}
field of the hit structure when the primitive is hit. See the
\hyperref[user-geometry]{User Geometry} tutorial for an example.

For multi-segment motion blur, the number of time steps must be first
specified using the \texttt{rtcSetGeometryTimeStepCount} function. Then
a transformation for each time step can be specified using the
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryTransform} function.

See tutorials \hyperref[instanced-geometry]{Instanced Geometry} and
\hyperref[multi-level-instancing]{Multi Level Instancing} for examples
of how to use instances.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure \texttt{NULL} is returned and an error code is set that can
be queried using \texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewgeometry]{rtcNewGeometry},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryinstancedscene]{rtcSetGeometryInstancedScene},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrytransform]{rtcSetGeometryTransform}

\pagebreak

\section{RTCCurveFlags}\label{rtccurveflags}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTCCurveFlags }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ per segment flags }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ curve geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCCurveFlags}
\OperatorTok{\{}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_CURVE\_FLAG\_NEIGHBOR\_LEFT  }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{(}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{),} 
\NormalTok{  RTC\_CURVE\_FLAG\_NEIGHBOR\_RIGHT }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{(}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{1}\OperatorTok{)} 
\OperatorTok{\};}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The RTCCurveFlags type is used for linear curves to determine if the
left and/or right neighbor segment exist. Therefore one attaches a
buffer of type RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_FLAGS to the curve geometry which
stores an individual byte per curve segment.

If the RTC\_CURVE\_FLAG\_NEIGHBOR\_LEFT flag in that byte is enabled for
a curve segment, then the left segment exists (which starts one vertex
before the start vertex of the current curve) and the current segment is
rendered to properly attach to that segment.

If the RTC\_CURVE\_FLAG\_NEIGHBOR\_RIGHT flag in that byte is enabled
for a curve segment, then the right segment exists (which ends one
vertex after the end vertex of the current curve) and the current
segment is rendered to properly attach to that segment.

When not properly specifying left and right flags for linear curves, the
rendering at the ending of these curves may not look correct, in
particular when round linear curves are viewed from the inside.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_curve]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_CURVE}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcRetainGeometry}\label{rtcretaingeometry}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcRetainGeometry }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ increments the geometry reference count}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcRetainGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

Geometry objects are reference counted. The \texttt{rtcRetainGeometry}
function increments the reference count of the passed geometry object
(\texttt{geometry} argument). This function together with
\texttt{rtcReleaseGeometry} allows to use the internal reference
counting in a C++ wrapper class to handle the ownership of the object.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewgeometry]{rtcNewGeometry},
\hyperref[rtcreleasegeometry]{rtcReleaseGeometry}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcReleaseGeometry}\label{rtcreleasegeometry}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcReleaseGeometry }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ decrements the geometry reference count}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcReleaseGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

Geometry objects are reference counted. The \texttt{rtcReleaseGeometry}
function decrements the reference count of the passed geometry object
(\texttt{geometry} argument). When the reference count falls to 0, the
geometry gets destroyed.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewgeometry]{rtcNewGeometry},
\hyperref[rtcretaingeometry]{rtcRetainGeometry}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcCommitGeometry}\label{rtccommitgeometry}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcCommitGeometry }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ commits geometry changes}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcCommitGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcCommitGeometry} function is used to commit all geometry
changes performed to a geometry (\texttt{geometry} parameter). After a
geometry gets modified, this function must be called to properly update
the internal state of the geometry to perform interpolations using
\texttt{rtcInterpolate} or to commit a scene containing the geometry
using \texttt{rtcCommitScene}.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcinterpolate]{rtcInterpolate},
\hyperref[rtccommitscene]{rtcCommitScene}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcEnableGeometry}\label{rtcenablegeometry}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcEnableGeometry }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ enables the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcEnableGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcEnableGeometry} function enables the specified geometry
(\texttt{geometry} argument). Only enabled geometries are rendered. Each
geometry is enabled by default at construction time.

After enabling a geometry, the scene containing that geometry must be
committed using \texttt{rtcCommitScene} for the change to have effect.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewgeometry]{rtcNewGeometry},
\hyperref[rtcdisablegeometry]{rtcDisableGeometry},
\hyperref[rtccommitscene]{rtcCommitScene}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcDisableGeometry}\label{rtcdisablegeometry}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcDisableGeometry }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ disables the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcDisableGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcDisableGeometry} function disables the specified geometry
(\texttt{geometry} argument). A disabled geometry is not rendered. Each
geometry is enabled by default at construction time.

After disabling a geometry, the scene containing that geometry must be
committed using \texttt{rtcCommitScene} for the change to have effect.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewgeometry]{rtcNewGeometry},
\hyperref[rtcenablegeometry]{rtcEnableGeometry},
\hyperref[rtccommitscene]{rtcCommitScene}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryTimeStepCount}\label{rtcsetgeometrytimestepcount}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryTimeStepCount }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the number of time steps of the}
\NormalTok{  geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryTimeStepCount}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ timeStepCount}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryTimeStepCount} function sets the number of
time steps for multi-segment motion blur (\texttt{timeStepCount}
parameter) of the specified geometry (\texttt{geometry} parameter).

For triangle meshes (\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_TRIANGLE}), quad
meshes (\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_QUAD}), curves
(\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_CURVE}), points
(\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_POINT}), and subdivision geometries
(\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_SUBDIVISION}), the number of time steps
directly corresponds to the number of vertex buffer slots available
(\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX} buffer type). For these geometries,
one vertex buffer per time step must be specified when creating
multi-segment motion blur geometries.

For instance geometries (\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_INSTANCE}), a
transformation must be specified for each time step (see
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryTransform}).

For user geometries, the registered bounding callback function must
provide a bounding box per primitive and time step, and the intersection
and occlusion callback functions should properly intersect the
motion-blurred geometry at the ray time.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewgeometry]{rtcNewGeometry},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrytimerange]{rtcSetGeometryTimeRange}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryTimeRange}\label{rtcsetgeometrytimerange}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryTimeRange }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the time range }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ a motion blur geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryTimeRange}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ startTime}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ endTime}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryTimeRange} function sets a time range which
defines the start (and end time) of the first (and last) time step of a
motion blur geometry. The time range is defined relative to the camera
shutter interval {[}0,1{]} but it can be arbitrary. Thus the startTime
can be smaller, equal, or larger 0, indicating a geometry whose
animation definition start before, at, or after the camera shutter
opens. Similar the endTime can be smaller, equal, or larger than 1,
indicating a geometry whose animation definition ends after, at, or
before the camera shutter closes. The startTime has to be smaller or
equal to the endTime.

The default time range when this function is not called is the entire
camera shutter {[}0,1{]}. For best performance at most one time segment
of the piece wise linear definition of the motion should fall outside
the shutter window to the left and to the right. Thus do not set the
startTime or endTime too far outside the {[}0,1{]} interval for best
performance.

This time range feature will also allow geometries to appear and
disappear during the camera shutter time if the specified time range is
a sub range of {[}0,1{]}.

Please also have a look at the \texttt{rtcSetGeometryTimeStepCount}
function to see how to define the time steps for the specified time
range.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrytimestepcount]{rtcSetGeometryTimeStepCount}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryVertexAttributeCount}\label{rtcsetgeometryvertexattributecount}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryVertexAttributeCount }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the number of vertex}
\NormalTok{  attributes of the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryVertexAttributeCount}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ vertexAttributeCount}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryVertexAttributeCount} function sets the number
of slots (\texttt{vertexAttributeCount} parameter) for vertex attribute
buffers (\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX\_ATTRIBUTE}) that can be used
for the specified geometry (\texttt{geometry} parameter).

This function is supported only for triangle meshes
(\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_TRIANGLE}), quad meshes
(\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_QUAD}), curves
(\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_CURVE}), points
(\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_POINT}), and subdivision geometries
(\texttt{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_SUBDIVISION}).

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewgeometry]{rtcNewGeometry},
\hyperref[rtcbuffertype]{RTCBufferType}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryMask}\label{rtcsetgeometrymask}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryMask }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the geometry mask}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryMask}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ mask}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryMask} function sets a 32-bit geometry mask
(\texttt{mask} argument) for the specified geometry (\texttt{geometry}
argument).

This geometry mask is used together with the ray mask stored inside the
\texttt{mask} field of the ray. The primitives of the geometry are hit
by the ray only if the bitwise \texttt{and} operation of the geometry
mask with the ray mask is not 0. This feature can be used to disable
selected geometries for specifically tagged rays, e.g.~to disable shadow
casting for certain geometries.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcray]{RTCRay},
\hyperref[rtcgetdeviceproperty]{rtcGetDeviceProperty}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryBuildQuality}\label{rtcsetgeometrybuildquality}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryBuildQuality }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the build quality }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryBuildQuality}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCBuildQuality quality}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryBuildQuality} function sets the build quality
(\texttt{quality} argument) for the specified geometry
(\texttt{geometry} argument). The per-geometry build quality is only a
hint and may be ignored. Embree currently uses the per-geometry build
quality when the scene build quality is set to
\texttt{RTC\_BUILD\_QUALITY\_LOW}. In this mode a two-level acceleration
structure is build, and geometries build a separate acceleration
structure using the geometry build quality. The per-geometry build
quality can be one of:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_BUILD\_QUALITY\_LOW}: Creates lower quality data
  structures, e.g.~for dynamic scenes.
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_BUILD\_QUALITY\_MEDIUM}: Default build quality for most
  usages. Gives a good compromise between build and render performance.
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_BUILD\_QUALITY\_HIGH}: Creates higher quality data
  structures for final-frame rendering. Enables a spatial split builder
  for certain primitive types.
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_BUILD\_QUALITY\_REFIT}: Uses a BVH refitting approach
  when changing only the vertex buffer.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetscenebuildquality]{rtcSetSceneBuildQuality}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryMaxRadiusScale}\label{rtcsetgeometrymaxradiusscale}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryMaxRadiusScale }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ assigns a maximal curve radius scale factor }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ min}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{width feature}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryMaxRadiusScale}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ maxRadiusScale}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetMaxGeometryScale} function specifies a maximal scaling
factor for curve radii used by the min-width feature.

The min-width feature can increase the radius of curves and points, in
order to reduce aliasing and improve render times. The feature is
disabled by default and has to get enabled using the EMBREE\_MIN\_WIDTH
cmake option.

To use the feature, one has to specify a maximal curve radius scaling
factor using the
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrymaxradiusscale]{rtcSetGeometryMaxRadiusScale}
function. This factor should be a small number (e.g.~4) as the
constructed BVH bounds get increased in order to bound the curve in the
worst case of maximal radii.

One also has to set the minWidthDistanceFactor in the RTCRayQueryContext
when tracing a ray. This factor controls the target radius size of a
curve or point at some distance away of the ray origin.

For each control point p with radius r of a curve or point primitive,
the primitive intersectors first calculate a target radius r' as:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{r}\CharTok{\textquotesingle{} }\ErrorTok{= length(p{-}ray\_org) * minWidthDistanceFactor}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Typically the minWidthDistanceFactor is set by the application such that
the target radius projects to the width of half a pixel (thus primitive
diameter is pixel sized).

The target radius r' is then clamped against the minimal bound r and
maximal bound maxRadiusScale*r to obtain the final radius r'\,':

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{r}\CharTok{\textquotesingle{}}\ErrorTok{\textquotesingle{}} \OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ max}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{r}\OperatorTok{,}\NormalTok{ min}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{r}\CharTok{\textquotesingle{},}\ErrorTok{ maxRadiusScale*r))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Thus curves or points close to the camera are rendered with a normal
radii r, and curves or points far from the camera are not enlarged too
much, as this would be very expensive to render.

When \texttt{rtcSetGeometryMaxRadiusScale} function is not invoked for a
curve or point geometry (or if the maximal scaling factor is set to
1.0), then the curve or point geometry renders normally, with radii not
modified by the min-width feature.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcinitrayquerycontext]{rtcInitRayQueryContext}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryBuffer}\label{rtcsetgeometrybuffer}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryBuffer }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ assigns a view of a buffer to the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryBuffer}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCBufferType type}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ slot}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCFormat format}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCBuffer buffer}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{size\_t}\NormalTok{ byteOffset}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{size\_t}\NormalTok{ byteStride}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{size\_t}\NormalTok{ itemCount}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryBuffer} function binds a view of a buffer
object (\texttt{buffer} argument) to a geometry buffer type and slot
(\texttt{type} and \texttt{slot} argument) of the specified geometry
(\texttt{geometry} argument).

One can specify the start of the first buffer element in bytes
(\texttt{byteOffset} argument), the byte stride between individual
buffer elements (\texttt{byteStride} argument), the format of the buffer
elements (\texttt{format} argument), and the number of elements to bind
(\texttt{itemCount}).

The start address (\texttt{byteOffset} argument) and stride
(\texttt{byteStride} argument) must be both aligned to 4 bytes,
otherwise the \texttt{rtcSetGeometryBuffer} function will fail.

After successful completion of this function, the geometry will hold a
reference to the buffer object.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetsharedgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcsetnewgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetNewGeometryBuffer}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer}\label{rtcsetsharedgeometrybuffer}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ assigns a view of a shared data buffer}
\NormalTok{  to a geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCBufferType type}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ slot}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCFormat format}\OperatorTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ptr}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{size\_t}\NormalTok{ byteOffset}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{size\_t}\NormalTok{ byteStride}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{size\_t}\NormalTok{ itemCount}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer} function binds a view of a
shared user-managed data buffer (\texttt{ptr} argument) to a geometry
buffer type and slot (\texttt{type} and \texttt{slot} argument) of the
specified geometry (\texttt{geometry} argument).

One can specify the start of the first buffer element in bytes
(\texttt{byteOffset} argument), the byte stride between individual
buffer elements (\texttt{byteStride} argument), the format of the buffer
elements (\texttt{format} argument), and the number of elements to bind
(\texttt{itemCount}).

The start address (\texttt{byteOffset} argument) and stride
(\texttt{byteStride} argument) must be both aligned to 4 bytes;
otherwise the \texttt{rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer} function will fail.

When the buffer will be used as a vertex buffer
(\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX} and
\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX\_ATTRIBUTE}), the last buffer element
must be readable using 16-byte SSE load instructions, thus padding the
last element is required for certain layouts. E.g. a standard
\texttt{float3} vertex buffer layout should add storage for at least one
more float to the end of the buffer.

The buffer data must remain valid for as long as the buffer may be used,
and the user is responsible for freeing the buffer data when no longer
required.

Sharing buffers can significantly reduce the memory required by the
application, thus we recommend using this feature. When enabling the
\texttt{RTC\_SCENE\_FLAG\_COMPACT} scene flag, the spatial index
structures index into the vertex buffer, resulting in even higher memory
savings.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetGeometryBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcsetnewgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetNewGeometryBuffer}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetNewGeometryBuffer}\label{rtcsetnewgeometrybuffer}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetNewGeometryBuffer }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ creates }\KeywordTok{and}\NormalTok{ assigns a }\KeywordTok{new}\NormalTok{ data buffer to}
\NormalTok{  the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rtcSetNewGeometryBuffer}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCBufferType type}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ slot}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCFormat format}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{size\_t}\NormalTok{ byteStride}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{size\_t}\NormalTok{ itemCount}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetNewGeometryBuffer} function creates a new data buffer
of specified format (\texttt{format} argument), byte stride
(\texttt{byteStride} argument), and number of items (\texttt{itemCount}
argument), and assigns it to a geometry buffer slot (\texttt{type} and
\texttt{slot} argument) of the specified geometry (\texttt{geometry}
argument). The buffer data is managed internally and automatically freed
when the geometry is destroyed.

The byte stride (\texttt{byteStride} argument) must be aligned to 4
bytes; otherwise the \texttt{rtcSetNewGeometryBuffer} function will
fail.

The allocated buffer will be automatically over-allocated slightly when
used as a vertex buffer, where a requirement is that each buffer element
should be readable using 16-byte SSE load instructions.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetGeometryBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcsetsharedgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer}

\pagebreak

\section{RTCFormat}\label{rtcformat}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTCFormat }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ specifies format of data in buffers}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore\_ray.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCFormat}
\OperatorTok{\{}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_UINT}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_UINT2}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_UINT3}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_UINT4}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT2}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT3}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT4}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT5}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT6}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT7}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT8}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT9}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT10}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT11}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT12}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT13}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT14}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT15}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT16}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT3X4\_ROW\_MAJOR}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT4X4\_ROW\_MAJOR}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT3X4\_COLUMN\_MAJOR}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT4X4\_COLUMN\_MAJOR}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FORMAT\_GRID}\OperatorTok{,}
\OperatorTok{\};}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{RTFormat} structure defines the data format stored in data
buffers provided to Embree using the
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetGeometryBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcsetsharedgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer}, and
\hyperref[rtcsetnewgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetNewGeometryBuffer} API calls.

The \texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_UINT/2/3/4} format are used to specify that
data buffers store unsigned integers, or unsigned integer vectors of
size 2,3 or 4. This format has typically to get used when specifying
index buffers, e.g.~\texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_UINT3} for triangle meshes.

The \texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT/2/3/4...} format are used to specify that
data buffers store single precision floating point values, or vectors
there of (size 2,3,4, etc.). This format is typcally used to specify to
format of vertex buffers, e.g.~the \texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT3} type for
vertex buffers of triangle meshes.

The \texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT3X4\_ROW\_MAJOR} and
\texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT3X4\_COLUMN\_MAJOR} formats, specify a 3x4
floating point matrix layed out either row major or column major. The
\texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT4X4\_ROW\_MAJOR} and
\texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT4X4\_COLUMN\_MAJOR} formats, specify a 4x4
floating point matrix layed out either row major or column major. These
matrix formats are used in the
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrytransform]{rtcSetGeometryTransform} function in
order to set a transformation matrix for geometries.

The \texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_GRID} is a special data format used to specify
grid primitives of layout RTCGrid when creating grid geometries (see
\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_grid]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_GRID}).

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetGeometryBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcsetsharedgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcsetnewgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetNewGeometryBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrytransform]{rtcSetGeometryTransform}

\pagebreak

\section{RTCBufferType}\label{rtcbuffertype}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTCFormat }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ specifies format of data in buffers}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore\_ray.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCBufferType}
\OperatorTok{\{}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_INDEX            }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX           }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX\_ATTRIBUTE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{2}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_NORMAL           }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{3}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_TANGENT          }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{4}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_NORMAL\_DERIVATIVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{5}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_GRID                 }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{8}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_FACE                 }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{16}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_LEVEL                }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{17}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_EDGE\_CREASE\_INDEX    }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{18}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_EDGE\_CREASE\_WEIGHT   }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{19}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX\_CREASE\_INDEX  }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{20}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX\_CREASE\_WEIGHT }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{21}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_HOLE                 }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{22}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_FLAGS }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{32}
\OperatorTok{\};}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{RTBufferType} structure defines slots to assign data buffers
to using the \hyperref[rtcsetgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetGeometryBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcsetsharedgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer}, and
\hyperref[rtcsetnewgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetNewGeometryBuffer} API calls.

For most geometry types the \texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_INDEX} slot is
used to assign an index buffer, while the
\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX} is used to assign the corresponding
vertex buffer.

The \texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX\_ATTRIBUTE} slot can get used to
assign arbitrary additional vertex data which can get interpolated using
the \hyperref[rtcinterpolate]{rtcInterpolate} API call.

The \texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_NORMAL},
\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_TANGENT}, and
\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_NORMAL\_DERIVATIVE} are special buffers
required to assign per vertex normals, tangents, and normal derivatives
for some curve types.

The \texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_GRID} buffer is used to assign the grid
primitive buffer for grid geometries (see
\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_grid]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_GRID}).

The \texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_FACE}, \texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_LEVEL},
\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_EDGE\_CREASE\_INDEX},
\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_EDGE\_CREASE\_WEIGHT},
\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX\_CREASE\_INDEX},
\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX\_CREASE\_WEIGHT}, and
\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_HOLE} are special buffers required to create
subdivision meshes (see
\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_subdivision]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_SUBDIVISION}).

The \texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_FLAGS} can get used to add additional
flag per primitive of a geometry, and is currently only used for linear
curves.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetGeometryBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcsetsharedgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcsetnewgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetNewGeometryBuffer}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcGetGeometryBufferData}\label{rtcgetgeometrybufferdata}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcGetGeometryBufferData }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ gets pointer to}
\NormalTok{  the first buffer view element}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rtcGetGeometryBufferData}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCBufferType type}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ slot}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcGetGeometryBufferData} function returns a pointer to the
first element of the buffer view attached to the specified buffer type
and slot (\texttt{type} and \texttt{slot} argument) of the geometry
(\texttt{geometry} argument).

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetGeometryBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcsetsharedgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetSharedGeometryBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcsetnewgeometrybuffer]{rtcSetNewGeometryBuffer}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcUpdateGeometryBuffer}\label{rtcupdategeometrybuffer}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcUpdateGeometryBuffer }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ marks a buffer view bound to the geometry}
\NormalTok{  as modified}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcUpdateGeometryBuffer}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCBufferType type}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ slot}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcUpdateGeometryBuffer} function marks the buffer view
bound to the specified buffer type and slot (\texttt{type} and
\texttt{slot} argument) of a geometry (\texttt{geometry} argument) as
modified.

If a data buffer is changed by the application, the
\texttt{rtcUpdateGeometryBuffer} call must be invoked for that buffer.
Each buffer view assigned to a buffer slot is initially marked as
modified, thus this function needs to be called only when doing buffer
modifications after the first \texttt{rtcCommitScene}.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewgeometry]{rtcNewGeometry},
\hyperref[rtccommitscene]{rtcCommitScene}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFilterFunction}\label{rtcsetgeometryintersectfilterfunction}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFilterFunction }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the intersection filter}
  \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCFilterFunctionNArguments}
\OperatorTok{\{}
  \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ valid}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ geometryUserPtr}\OperatorTok{;}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayQueryContext}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ context}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ hit}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{;}
\OperatorTok{\};}

\KeywordTok{typedef} \DataTypeTok{void} \OperatorTok{(*}\NormalTok{RTCFilterFunctionN}\OperatorTok{)(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCFilterFunctionNArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}
\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryIntersectFilterFunction}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCFilterFunctionN filter}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFilterFunction} function registers an
intersection filter callback function (\texttt{filter} argument) for the
specified geometry (\texttt{geometry} argument).

Only a single callback function can be registered per geometry, and
further invocations overwrite the previously set callback function.
Passing \texttt{NULL} as function pointer disables the registered
callback function.

The registered intersection filter function is invoked for every hit
encountered during the \texttt{rtcIntersect}-type ray queries and can
accept or reject that hit. The feature can be used to define a
silhouette for a primitive and reject hits that are outside the
silhouette. E.g. a tree leaf could be modeled with an alpha texture that
decides whether hit points lie inside or outside the leaf.

If the \texttt{RTC\_BUILD\_QUALITY\_HIGH} mode is set, the filter
functions may be called multiple times for the same primitive hit.
Further, rays hitting exactly the edge might also report two hits for
the same surface. For certain use cases, the application may have to
work around this limitation by collecting already reported hits
(\texttt{geomID}/\texttt{primID} pairs) and ignoring duplicates.

The filter function callback of type \texttt{RTCFilterFunctionN} gets
passed a number of arguments through the
\texttt{RTCFilterFunctionNArguments} structure. The \texttt{valid}
parameter of that structure points to an integer valid mask (0 means
invalid and -1 means valid). The \texttt{geometryUserPtr} member is a
user pointer optionally set per geometry through the
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryUserData} function. The \texttt{context} member
points to the ray query context passed to the ray query function. The
\texttt{ray} parameter points to \texttt{N} rays in SOA layout. The
\texttt{hit} parameter points to \texttt{N} hits in SOA layout to test.
The \texttt{N} parameter is the number of rays and hits in \texttt{ray}
and \texttt{hit}. The hit distance is provided as the \texttt{tfar}
value of the ray. If the hit geometry is instanced, the \texttt{instID}
member of the ray is valid, and the ray and the potential hit are in
object space.

The filter callback function has the task to check for each valid ray
whether it wants to accept or reject the corresponding hit. To reject a
hit, the filter callback function just has to write \texttt{0} to the
integer valid mask of the corresponding ray. To accept the hit, it just
has to leave the valid mask set to \texttt{-1}. The filter function is
further allowed to change the hit and decrease the \texttt{tfar} value
of the ray but it should not modify other ray data nor any inactive
components of the ray or hit.

When performing ray queries using \texttt{rtcIntersect1}, it is
guaranteed that the packet size is 1 when the callback is invoked. When
performing ray queries using the \texttt{rtcIntersect4/8/16} functions,
it is not generally guaranteed that the ray packet size (and order of
rays inside the packet) passed to the callback matches the initial ray
packet. However, under some circumstances these properties are
guaranteed, and whether this is the case can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceProperty}.

For many usage scenarios, repacking and re-ordering of rays does not
cause difficulties in implementing the callback function. However,
algorithms that need to extend the ray with additional data must use the
\texttt{rayID} component of the ray to identify the original ray to
access the per-ray data.

The implementation of the filter function can choose to implement a
single code path that uses the ray access helper functions
\texttt{RTCRay\_XXX} and hit access helper functions
\texttt{RTCHit\_XXX} to access ray and hit data. Alternatively the code
can branch to optimized implementations for specific sizes of \texttt{N}
and cast the \texttt{ray} and \texttt{hit} inputs to the proper packet
types.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryoccludedfilterfunction]{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFilterFunction}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFilterFunction}\label{rtcsetgeometryoccludedfilterfunction}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFilterFunction }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the occlusion filter}
  \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryOccludedFilterFunction}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCFilterFunctionN filter}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFilterFunction} function registers an
occlusion filter callback function (\texttt{filter} argument) for the
specified geometry (\texttt{geometry} argument).

Only a single callback function can be registered per geometry, and
further invocations overwrite the previously set callback function.
Passing \texttt{NULL} as function pointer disables the registered
callback function.

The registered intersection filter function is invoked for every hit
encountered during the \texttt{rtcOccluded}-type ray queries and can
accept or reject that hit. The feature can be used to define a
silhouette for a primitive and reject hits that are outside the
silhouette. E.g. a tree leaf could be modeled with an alpha texture that
decides whether hit points lie inside or outside the leaf.

Please see the description of the
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFilterFunction} for a description of the
filter callback function.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryintersectfilterfunction]{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFilterFunction}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryEnableFilterFunctionFromArguments}\label{rtcsetgeometryenablefilterfunctionfromarguments}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryEnableFilterFunctionFromArguments }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ enables}
\NormalTok{  argument filter functions }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryEnableFilterFunctionFromArguments}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{   RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{bool}\NormalTok{ enable}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

This function enables invokation the filter function passed through
\texttt{RTCIntersectArguments} or \texttt{RTCOccludedArguments} to the
intersect and occluded queries. If enable is true the argument filter
function invokation is enabled for the geometry or disabled otherwise.
By default the invokation of the argument filter function is disabled
for some geometry.

The argument filter function invokation can also get enforced for each
geometry by using the
\texttt{RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_INVOKE\_ARGUMENT\_FILTER} ray query flag
that can get passed to \texttt{rtcIntersect} and \texttt{rtcOccluded}
functions. See Section
\hyperref[rtcinitintersectarguments]{rtcInitIntersectArguments} and
\hyperref[rtcinitoccludedarguments]{rtcInitOccludedArguments} for more
details.

In order to use the argument filter function for some scene, that
feature additionally has to get enabled using the
\texttt{RTC\_SCENE\_FLAG\_FILTER\_FUNCTION\_IN\_ARGUMENTS} scene flag.
See Section \hyperref[rtcsetsceneflags]{rtcSetSceneFlags} for more
details.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can get queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcinitintersectarguments]{rtcInitIntersectArguments},
\hyperref[rtcinitoccludedarguments]{rtcInitOccludedArguments},
\hyperref[rtcsetsceneflags]{rtcSetSceneFlags}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcInvokeIntersectFilterFromGeometry}\label{rtcinvokeintersectfilterfromgeometry}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcInvokeIntersectFilterFromGeometry }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ invokes the}
\NormalTok{  intersection filter function from the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcInvokeIntersectFilterFromGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCIntersectFunctionNArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}\OperatorTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCFilterFunctionNArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ filterArgs}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcInvokeIntersectFilterFromGeometry} function can be called
inside an \texttt{RTCIntersectFunctionN} user geometry callback function
to invoke the intersection filter registered to the geometry. For this
an \texttt{RTCFilterFunctionNArguments} structure must be created (see
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFilterFunction}) which basically consists
of a valid mask, a hit packet to filter, the corresponding ray packet,
and the packet size. After the invocation of
\texttt{rtcInvokeIntersectFilterFromGeometry}, only rays that are still
valid (valid mask set to -1) should update a hit.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

For performance reasons this function does not do any error checks, thus
will not set any error flags on failure.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcinvokeoccludedfilterfromgeometry]{rtcInvokeOccludedFilterFromGeometry},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryintersectfunction]{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFunction}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcInvokeOccludedFilterFromGeometry}\label{rtcinvokeoccludedfilterfromgeometry}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcInvokeOccludedFilterFromGeometry }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ invokes the occlusion}
\NormalTok{  filter function from the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcInvokeOccludedFilterFromGeometry}\OperatorTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCOccludedFunctionNArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}\OperatorTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCFilterFunctionNArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ filterArgs}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcInvokeOccludedFilterFromGeometry} function can be called
inside an \texttt{RTCOccludedFunctionN} user geometry callback function
to invoke the occlusion filter registered to the geometry. For this an
\texttt{RTCFilterFunctionNArguments} structure must be created (see
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFilterFunction}) which basically consists
of a valid mask, a hit packet to filter, the corresponding ray packet,
and the packet size. After the invocation of
\texttt{rtcInvokeOccludedFilterFromGeometry} only rays that are still
valid (valid mask set to -1) should signal an occlusion.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

For performance reasons this function does not do any error checks, thus
will not set any error flags on failure.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcinvokeintersectfilterfromgeometry]{rtcInvokeIntersectFilterFromGeometry},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryoccludedfunction]{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFunction}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryUserData}\label{rtcsetgeometryuserdata}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryUserData }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the user}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{defined data pointer of the}
\NormalTok{  geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryUserData}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ userPtr}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryUserData} function sets the user-defined data
pointer (\texttt{userPtr} argument) for a geometry (\texttt{geometry}
argument). This user data pointer is intended to be pointing to the
application's representation of the geometry, and is passed to various
callback functions. The application can use this pointer inside the
callback functions to access its geometry representation.

The \texttt{rtcGetGeometryUserData} function can be used to query an
already set user data pointer of a geometry.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcgetgeometryuserdata]{rtcGetGeometryUserData}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcGetGeometryUserData}\label{rtcgetgeometryuserdata}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcGetGeometryUserData }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ returns the user data pointer}
\NormalTok{  of the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rtcGetGeometryUserData}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcGetGeometryUserData} function queries the user data
pointer previously set with \texttt{rtcSetGeometryUserData}. When
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryUserData} was not called yet, \texttt{NULL} is
returned.

This function is supposed to be used during rendering, but only
supported on the CPU and in SYCL on the GPU.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryuserdata]{rtcSetGeometryUserData}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcGetGeometryUserDataFromScene}\label{rtcgetgeometryuserdatafromscene}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcGetGeometryUserDataFromScene }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ returns the user data pointer}
\NormalTok{  of the geometry through the scene object}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rtcGetGeometryUserDataFromScene}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCScene scene}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ geomID}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcGetGeometryUserDataFromScene} function queries the user
data pointer previously set with \texttt{rtcSetGeometryUserData} from
the geometry with index \texttt{geomID} from the specified scene
\texttt{scene}. When \texttt{rtcSetGeometryUserData} was not called yet,
\texttt{NULL} is returned.

This function is supposed to be used during rendering.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryuserdata]{rtcSetGeometryUserData},
\hyperref[rtcgetgeometryuserdata]{rtcGetGeometryUserData}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryUserPrimitiveCount}\label{rtcsetgeometryuserprimitivecount}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryUserPrimitiveCount }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the number of primitives}
\NormalTok{  of a user}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{defined geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryUserPrimitiveCount}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ userPrimitiveCount}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryUserPrimitiveCount} function sets the number
of user-defined primitives (\texttt{userPrimitiveCount} parameter) of
the specified user-defined geometry (\texttt{geometry} parameter).

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_user]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_USER}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryBoundsFunction}\label{rtcsetgeometryboundsfunction}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryBoundsFunction }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets a callback to query the}
\NormalTok{  bounding box of user}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{defined primitives}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCBoundsFunctionArguments}
\OperatorTok{\{}
  \DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ geometryUserPtr}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ primID}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ timeStep}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCBounds}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ bounds\_o}\OperatorTok{;}
\OperatorTok{\};}

\KeywordTok{typedef} \DataTypeTok{void} \OperatorTok{(*}\NormalTok{RTCBoundsFunction}\OperatorTok{)(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCBoundsFunctionArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}
\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryBoundsFunction}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCBoundsFunction bounds}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ userPtr}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryBoundsFunction} function registers a bounding
box callback function (\texttt{bounds} argument) with payload
(\texttt{userPtr} argument) for the specified user geometry
(\texttt{geometry} argument).

Only a single callback function can be registered per geometry, and
further invocations overwrite the previously set callback function.
Passing \texttt{NULL} as function pointer disables the registered
callback function.

In SYCL mode the BVH construction is done on the host and the passed
function pointer must be a host-side function pointer.

The registered bounding box callback function is invoked to calculate
axis-aligned bounding boxes of the primitives of the user-defined
geometry during spatial acceleration structure construction. The
bounding box callback of \texttt{RTCBoundsFunction} type is invoked with
a pointer to a structure of type \texttt{RTCBoundsFunctionArguments}
which contains various arguments, such as: the user data of the geometry
(\texttt{geometryUserPtr} member), the ID of the primitive to calculate
the bounds for (\texttt{primID} member), the time step at which to
calculate the bounds (\texttt{timeStep} member), and a memory location
to write the calculated bound to (\texttt{bounds\_o} member).

In a typical usage scenario one would store a pointer to the internal
representation of the user geometry object using
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryUserData}. The callback function can then read
that pointer from the \texttt{geometryUserPtr} field and calculate the
proper bounding box for the requested primitive and time, and store that
bounding box to the destination structure (\texttt{bounds\_o} member).

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_user]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_USER}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFunction}\label{rtcsetgeometryintersectfunction}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFunction }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the callback function to}
\NormalTok{  intersect a user geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCIntersectFunctionNArguments}
\OperatorTok{\{}
  \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ valid}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ geometryUserPtr}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ primID}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayQueryContext}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ context}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rayhit}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ geomID}\OperatorTok{;}
\OperatorTok{\};}

\KeywordTok{typedef} \DataTypeTok{void} \OperatorTok{(*}\NormalTok{RTCIntersectFunctionN}\OperatorTok{)(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCIntersectFunctionNArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}
\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryIntersectFunction}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCIntersectFunctionN intersect}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFunction} function registers a
ray/primitive intersection callback function (\texttt{intersect}
argument) for the specified user geometry (\texttt{geometry} argument).

Only a single callback function can be registered per geometry and
further invocations overwrite the previously set callback function.
Passing \texttt{NULL} as function pointer disables the registered
callback function.

The registered callback function is invoked by
\texttt{rtcIntersect}-type ray queries to calculate the intersection of
a ray packet of variable size with one user-defined primitive. The
callback function of type \texttt{RTCIntersectFunctionN} gets passed a
number of arguments through the \texttt{RTCIntersectFunctionNArguments}
structure. The value \texttt{N} specifies the ray packet size,
\texttt{valid} points to an array of integers that specify whether the
corresponding ray is valid (-1) or invalid (0), the
\texttt{geometryUserPtr} member points to the geometry user data
previously set through \texttt{rtcSetGeometryUserData}, the
\texttt{context} member points to the ray query context passed to the
ray query, the \texttt{rayhit} member points to a ray and hit packet of
variable size \texttt{N}, and the \texttt{geomID} and \texttt{primID}
member identifies the geometry ID and primitive ID of the primitive to
intersect.

The \texttt{ray} component of the \texttt{rayhit} structure contains
valid data, in particular the \texttt{tfar} value is the current closest
hit distance found. All data inside the \texttt{hit} component of the
\texttt{rayhit} structure are undefined and should not be read by the
function.

The task of the callback function is to intersect each active ray from
the ray packet with the specified user primitive. If the user-defined
primitive is missed by a ray of the ray packet, the function should
return without modifying the ray or hit. If an intersection of the
user-defined primitive with the ray was found in the valid range (from
\texttt{tnear} to \texttt{tfar}), it should update the hit distance of
the ray (\texttt{tfar} member) and the hit (\texttt{u}, \texttt{v},
\texttt{Ng}, \texttt{instID}, \texttt{geomID}, \texttt{primID} members).
In particular, the currently intersected instance is stored in the
\texttt{instID} field of the ray query context, which must be deep
copied into the \texttt{instID} member of the hit.

As a primitive might have multiple intersections with a ray, the
intersection filter function needs to be invoked by the user geometry
intersection callback for each encountered intersection, if filtering of
intersections is desired. This can be achieved through the
\texttt{rtcInvokeIntersectFilterFromGeometry} call.

Within the user geometry intersect function, it is safe to trace new
rays and create new scenes and geometries.

When performing ray queries using \texttt{rtcIntersect1}, it is
guaranteed that the packet size is 1 when the callback is invoked. When
performing ray queries using the \texttt{rtcIntersect4/8/16} functions,
it is not generally guaranteed that the ray packet size (and order of
rays inside the packet) passed to the callback matches the initial ray
packet. However, under some circumstances these properties are
guaranteed, and whether this is the case can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceProperty}.

For many usage scenarios, repacking and re-ordering of rays does not
cause difficulties in implementing the callback function. However,
algorithms that need to extend the ray with additional data must use the
\texttt{rayID} component of the ray to identify the original ray to
access the per-ray data.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryoccludedfunction]{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFunction},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryuserdata]{rtcSetGeometryUserData},
\hyperref[rtcinvokeintersectfilterfromgeometry]{rtcInvokeIntersectFilterFromGeometry}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFunction}\label{rtcsetgeometryoccludedfunction}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFunction }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the callback function to}
\NormalTok{  test a user geometry }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ occlusion}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCOccludedFunctionNArguments}
\OperatorTok{\{}
  \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ valid}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ geometryUserPtr}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ primID}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayQueryContext}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ context}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ geomID}\OperatorTok{;}
\OperatorTok{\};}
