\KeywordTok{typedef} \DataTypeTok{void} \OperatorTok{(*}\NormalTok{RTCOccludedFunctionN}\OperatorTok{)(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCOccludedFunctionNArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}
\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryOccludedFunction}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCOccludedFunctionN filter}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFunction} function registers a
ray/primitive occlusion callback function (\texttt{filter} argument) for
the specified user geometry (\texttt{geometry} argument).

Only a single callback function can be registered per geometry, and
further invocations overwrite the previously set callback function.
Passing \texttt{NULL} as function pointer disables the registered
callback function.

The registered callback function is invoked by \texttt{rtcOccluded}-type
ray queries to test whether the rays of a packet of variable size are
occluded by a user-defined primitive. The callback function of type
\texttt{RTCOccludedFunctionN} gets passed a number of arguments through
the \texttt{RTCOccludedFunctionNArguments} structure. The value
\texttt{N} specifies the ray packet size, \texttt{valid} points to an
array of integers which specify whether the corresponding ray is valid
(-1) or invalid (0), the \texttt{geometryUserPtr} member points to the
geometry user data previously set through
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryUserData}, the \texttt{context} member points to
the ray query context passed to the ray query, the \texttt{ray} member
points to a ray packet of variable size \texttt{N}, and the
\texttt{geomID} and \texttt{primID} member identifies the geometry ID
and primitive ID of the primitive to intersect.

The task of the callback function is to intersect each active ray from
the ray packet with the specified user primitive. If the user-defined
primitive is missed by a ray of the ray packet, the function should
return without modifying the ray. If an intersection of the user-defined
primitive with the ray was found in the valid range (from \texttt{tnear}
to \texttt{tfar}), it should set the \texttt{tfar} member of the ray to
\texttt{-inf}.

As a primitive might have multiple intersections with a ray, the
occlusion filter function needs to be invoked by the user geometry
occlusion callback for each encountered intersection, if filtering of
intersections is desired. This can be achieved through the
\texttt{rtcInvokeOccludedFilterFromGeometry} call.

Within the user geometry occlusion function, it is safe to trace new
rays and create new scenes and geometries.

When performing ray queries using \texttt{rtcOccluded1}, it is
guaranteed that the packet size is 1 when the callback is invoked. When
performing ray queries using the \texttt{rtcOccluded4/8/16} functions,
it is not generally guaranteed that the ray packet size (and order of
rays inside the packet) passed to the callback matches the initial ray
packet. However, under some circumstances these properties are
guaranteed, and whether this is the case can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceProperty}.

For many usage scenarios, repacking and re-ordering of rays does not
cause difficulties in implementing the callback function. However,
algorithms that need to extend the ray with additional data must use the
\texttt{rayID} component of the ray to identify the original ray to
access the per-ray data.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryintersectfunction]{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFunction},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryuserdata]{rtcSetGeometryUserData},
\hyperref[rtcinvokeoccludedfilterfromgeometry]{rtcInvokeOccludedFilterFromGeometry}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryPointQueryFunction}\label{rtcsetgeometrypointqueryfunction}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryPointQueryFunction }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the point query callback function}
  \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ a geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCPointQueryFunctionArguments}
\OperatorTok{\{}
  \CommentTok{// the (world space) query object that was passed as an argument of rtcPointQuery.}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCPointQuery}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ query}\OperatorTok{;}

  \CommentTok{// used for user input/output data. Will not be read or modified internally.}
  \DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ userPtr}\OperatorTok{;}

  \CommentTok{// primitive and geometry ID of primitive}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{  primID}\OperatorTok{;}        
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{  geomID}\OperatorTok{;}    

  \CommentTok{// the context with transformation and instance ID stack}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCPointQueryContext}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ context}\OperatorTok{;}

  \CommentTok{// scaling factor indicating whether the current instance transformation}
  \CommentTok{// is a similarity transformation.}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ similarityScale}\OperatorTok{;}
\OperatorTok{\};}

\KeywordTok{typedef} \DataTypeTok{bool} \OperatorTok{(*}\NormalTok{RTCPointQueryFunction}\OperatorTok{)(}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCPointQueryFunctionArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}
\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryPointQueryFunction}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCPointQueryFunction queryFunc}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryPointQueryFunction} function registers a point
query callback function (\texttt{queryFunc} argument) for the specified
geometry (\texttt{geometry} argument).

Only a single callback function can be registered per geometry and
further invocations overwrite the previously set callback function.
Passing \texttt{NULL} as function pointer disables the registered
callback function.

The registered callback function is invoked by
\hyperref[rtcpointquery]{rtcPointQuery} for every primitive of the
geometry that intersects the corresponding point query domain. The
callback function of type \texttt{RTCPointQueryFunction} gets passed a
number of arguments through the \texttt{RTCPointQueryFunctionArguments}
structure. The \texttt{query} object is the original point query object
passed into \hyperref[rtcpointquery]{rtcPointQuery}, \texttt{usrPtr} is
an arbitrary pointer to pass input into and store results of the
callback function. The \texttt{primID}, \texttt{geomID} and
\texttt{context} (see
\hyperref[rtcinitpointquerycontext]{rtcInitPointQueryContext} for
details) can be used to identify the geometry data of the primitive.

A \texttt{RTCPointQueryFunction} can also be passed directly as an
argument to \hyperref[rtcpointquery]{rtcPointQuery}. In this case the
callback is invoked for all primitives in the scene that intersect the
query domain. If a callback function is passed as an argument to
\hyperref[rtcpointquery]{rtcPointQuery} and (a potentially different)
callback function is set for a geometry with
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrypointqueryfunction]{rtcSetGeometryPointQueryFunction}
both callback functions are invoked and the callback function passed to
\hyperref[rtcpointquery]{rtcPointQuery} will be called before the
geometry specific callback function.

If instancing is used, the parameter \texttt{simliarityScale} indicates
whether the current instance transform (top element of the stack in
\texttt{context}) is a similarity transformation or not. Similarity
transformations are composed of translation, rotation and uniform
scaling and if a matrix M defines a similarity transformation, there is
a scaling factor D such that for all x,y: dist(Mx, My) = D * dist(x, y).
In this case the parameter \texttt{scalingFactor} is this scaling factor
D and otherwise it is 0. A valid similarity scale
(\texttt{similarityScale} \textgreater{} 0) allows to compute distance
information in instance space and scale the distances into world space
(for example, to update the query radius, see below) by dividing the
instance space distance with the similarity scale. If the current
instance transform is not a similarity transform
(\texttt{similarityScale} is 0), the distance computation has to be
performed in world space to ensure correctness. In this case the
instance to world transformations given with the \texttt{context} should
be used to transform the primitive data into world space. Otherwise, the
query location can be transformed into instance space which can be more
efficient. If there is no instance transform, the similarity scale is 1.

The callback function will potentially be called for primitives outside
the query domain for two reasons: First, the callback is invoked for all
primitives inside a BVH leaf node since no geometry data of primitives
is determined internally and therefore individual primitives are not
culled (only their (aggregated) bounding boxes). Second, in case non
similarity transformations are used, the resulting ellipsoidal query
domain (in instance space) is approximated by its axis aligned bounding
box internally and therefore inner nodes that do not intersect the
original domain might intersect the approximative bounding box which
results in unnecessary callbacks. In any case, the callbacks are
conservative, i.e.~if a primitive is inside the query domain a callback
will be invoked but the reverse is not necessarily true.

For efficiency, the radius of the \texttt{query} object can be decreased
(in world space) inside the callback function to improve culling of
geometry during BVH traversal. If the query radius was updated, the
callback function should return \texttt{true} to issue an update of
internal traversal information. Increasing the radius or modifying the
time or position of the query results in undefined behaviour.

Within the callback function, it is safe to call
\hyperref[rtcpointquery]{rtcPointQuery} again, for example when
implementing instancing manually. In this case the instance
transformation should be pushed onto the stack in \texttt{context}.
Embree will internally compute the point query information in instance
space using the top element of the stack in \texttt{context} when
\hyperref[rtcpointquery]{rtcPointQuery} is called.

For a reference implementation of a closest point traversal of triangle
meshes using instancing and user defined instancing see the tutorial
{[}ClosestPoint{]}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcpointquery]{rtcPointQuery},
\hyperref[rtcinitpointquerycontext]{rtcInitPointQueryContext}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcGetSYCLDeviceFunctionPointer}\label{rtcgetsycldevicefunctionpointer}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcGetSYCLDeviceFunctionPointer }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ obtains a device side}
\NormalTok{  function pointer }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ some SYCL function}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{template}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\KeywordTok{auto}\NormalTok{ F}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}
\KeywordTok{inline} \KeywordTok{decltype}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{F}\OperatorTok{)}\NormalTok{ rtcGetSYCLDeviceFunctionPointer}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{sycl}\OperatorTok{::}\NormalTok{queue}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ queue}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

This function returns a device side function pointer for some function
F. This function F must be defined using the
\texttt{RTC\_SYCL\_INDIRECTLY\_CALLABLE} attribute, e.g.:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTC\_SYCL\_INDIRECTLY\_CALLABLE }\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ filter}\OperatorTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{const}\NormalTok{ RTCFilterFunctionNArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}\OperatorTok{)} \OperatorTok{\{} \OperatorTok{...} \OperatorTok{\}}

\NormalTok{RTCFilterFunctionN fptr }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rtcGetSYCLDeviceFunctionPointer}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\NormalTok{filter}\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}(}\NormalTok{queue}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Such a device side function pointers of some filter callbacks can get
assigned to a geometry using the
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFilterFunction} and
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFilterFunction} API functions.

Further, device side function pointers for user geometry callbacks can
be assigned to geometries using the
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFunction} and
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFunction} API calls.

These geometry versions of the callback functions are disabled in SYCL
by default, and we recommend not using them for performance reasons.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryintersectfunction]{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFunction},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryoccludedfunction]{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFunction},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryintersectfilterfunction]{rtcSetGeometryIntersectFilterFunction},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometryoccludedfilterfunction]{rtcSetGeometryOccludedFilterFunction}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryInstancedScene}\label{rtcsetgeometryinstancedscene}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryInstancedScene }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the instanced scene of}
\NormalTok{  an instance geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryInstancedScene}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCScene scene}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryInstancedScene} function sets the instanced
scene (\texttt{scene} argument) of the specified instance geometry
(\texttt{geometry} argument).

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_instance]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_INSTANCE},

\section{rtcSetGeometryTransform}\label{rtcsetgeometrytransform}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryTransform }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the transformation }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ a particular}
\NormalTok{  time step of an instance geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryTransform}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ timeStep}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCFormat format}\OperatorTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ xfm}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryTransform} function sets the local-to-world
affine transformation (\texttt{xfm} parameter) of an instance geometry
(\texttt{geometry} parameter) for a particular time step
(\texttt{timeStep} parameter). The transformation is specified as a 3×4
matrix (3×3 linear transformation plus translation), for which the
following formats (\texttt{format} parameter) are supported:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT3X4\_ROW\_MAJOR}: The 3×4 float matrix is
  laid out in row-major form.
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT3X4\_COLUMN\_MAJOR}: The 3×4 float matrix is
  laid out in column-major form.
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT4X4\_COLUMN\_MAJOR}: The 3×4 float matrix is
  laid out in column-major form as a 4×4 homogeneous matrix with the
  last row being equal to (0, 0, 0, 1).
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_instance]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_INSTANCE}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryTransformQuaternion}\label{rtcsetgeometrytransformquaternion}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryTransformQuaternion }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the transformation }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ a particular}
\NormalTok{  time step of an instance geometry as a decomposition of the}
\NormalTok{  transformation matrix }\KeywordTok{using}\NormalTok{ quaternions to represent the rotation}\OperatorTok{.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryTransformQuaternion}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ timeStep}\OperatorTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCQuaternionDecomposition}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ qd}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryTransformQuaternion} function sets the
local-to-world affine transformation (\texttt{qd} parameter) of an
instance geometry (\texttt{geometry} parameter) for a particular time
step (\texttt{timeStep} parameter). The transformation is specified as a
\hyperref[rtcquaterniondecomposition]{RTCQuaternionDecomposition}, which
is a decomposition of an affine transformation that represents the
rotational component of an affine transformation as a quaternion. This
allows interpolating rotational transformations exactly using spherical
linear interpolation (such as a turning wheel).

For more information about the decomposition see
\hyperref[rtcquaterniondecomposition]{RTCQuaternionDecomposition}. The
quaternion given in the \texttt{RTCQuaternionDecomposition} struct will
be normalized internally.

For correct results, the transformation matrices for all time steps must
be set either using \texttt{rtcSetGeometryTransform} or
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryTransformQuaternion}. Mixing both representations
is not allowed. Spherical linear interpolation will be used, iff the
transformation matizes are set with
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryTransformQuaternion}.

For an example of this feature see the tutorial
\hyperref[quaternion-motion-blur]{Quaternion Motion Blur}.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcinitquaterniondecomposition]{rtcInitQuaternionDecomposition},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrytransform]{rtcSetGeometryTransform}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcGetGeometryTransform}\label{rtcgetgeometrytransform}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcGetGeometryTransform }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ returns the interpolated instance}
\NormalTok{  transformation }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ the specified time}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcGetGeometryTransform}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ time}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCFormat format}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ xfm}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcGetGeometryTransform} function returns the interpolated
local to world transformation (\texttt{xfm} parameter) of an instance
geometry (\texttt{geometry} parameter) for a particular time
(\texttt{time} parameter in range \([0,1]\)) in the specified format
(\texttt{format} parameter).

Possible formats for the returned matrix are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT3X4\_ROW\_MAJOR}: The 3×4 float matrix is
  laid out in row-major form.
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT3X4\_COLUMN\_MAJOR}: The 3×4 float matrix is
  laid out in column-major form.
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_FORMAT\_FLOAT4X4\_COLUMN\_MAJOR}: The 3×4 float matrix is
  laid out in column-major form as a 4×4 homogeneous matrix with last
  row equal to (0, 0, 0, 1).
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_instance]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_INSTANCE},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrytransform]{rtcSetGeometryTransform}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryTessellationRate}\label{rtcsetgeometrytessellationrate}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryTessellationRate }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the tessellation rate of the}
\NormalTok{  geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryTessellationRate}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ tessellationRate}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryTessellationRate} function sets the
tessellation rate (\texttt{tessellationRate} argument) for the specified
geometry (\texttt{geometry} argument). The tessellation rate can only be
set for flat curves and subdivision geometries. For curves, the
tessellation rate specifies the number of ray-facing quads per curve
segment. For subdivision surfaces, the tessellation rate specifies the
number of quads along each edge.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_curve]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_CURVE},
\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_subdivision]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_SUBDIVISION}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryTopologyCount}\label{rtcsetgeometrytopologycount}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryTopologyCount }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the number of topologies of}
\NormalTok{  a subdivision geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryTopologyCount}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ topologyCount}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryTopologyCount} function sets the number of
topologies (\texttt{topologyCount} parameter) for the specified
subdivision geometry (\texttt{geometry} parameter). The number of
topologies of a subdivision geometry must be greater or equal to 1.

To use multiple topologies, first the number of topologies must be
specified, then the individual topologies can be configured using
\texttt{rtcSetGeometrySubdivisionMode} and by setting an index buffer
(\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_INDEX}) using the topology ID as the buffer
slot.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_subdivision]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_SUBDIVISION},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrysubdivisionmode]{rtcSetGeometrySubdivisionMode}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometrySubdivisionMode}\label{rtcsetgeometrysubdivisionmode}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometrySubdivisionMode }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the subdivision mode}
\NormalTok{  of a subdivision geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometrySubdivisionMode}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ topologyID}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCSubdivisionMode mode}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometrySubdivisionMode} function sets the subdivision
mode (\texttt{mode} parameter) for the topology (\texttt{topologyID}
parameter) of the specified subdivision geometry (\texttt{geometry}
parameter).

The subdivision modes can be used to force linear interpolation for
certain parts of the subdivision mesh:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_SUBDIVISION\_MODE\_NO\_BOUNDARY}: Boundary patches are
  ignored. This way each rendered patch has a full set of control
  vertices.
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_SUBDIVISION\_MODE\_SMOOTH\_BOUNDARY}: The sequence of
  boundary control points are used to generate a smooth B-spline
  boundary curve (default mode).
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_SUBDIVISION\_MODE\_PIN\_CORNERS}: Corner vertices are
  pinned to their location during subdivision.
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_SUBDIVISION\_MODE\_PIN\_BOUNDARY}: All vertices at the
  border are pinned to their location during subdivision. This way the
  boundary is interpolated linearly. This mode is typically used for
  texturing to also map texels at the border of the texture to the mesh.
\item
  \texttt{RTC\_SUBDIVISION\_MODE\_PIN\_ALL}: All vertices at the border
  are pinned to their location during subdivision. This way all patches
  are linearly interpolated.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_subdivision]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_SUBDIVISION}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryVertexAttributeTopology}\label{rtcsetgeometryvertexattributetopology}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryVertexAttributeTopology }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ binds a vertex}
\NormalTok{  attribute to a topology of the geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryVertexAttributeTopology}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ vertexAttributeID}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ topologyID}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryVertexAttributeTopology} function binds a
vertex attribute buffer slot (\texttt{vertexAttributeID} argument) to a
topology (\texttt{topologyID} argument) for the specified subdivision
geometry (\texttt{geometry} argument). Standard vertex buffers are
always bound to the default topology (topology 0) and cannot be bound
differently. A vertex attribute buffer always uses the topology it is
bound to when used in the \texttt{rtcInterpolate} and
\texttt{rtcInterpolateN} calls.

A topology with ID \texttt{i} consists of a subdivision mode set through
\texttt{rtcSetGeometrySubdivisionMode} and the index buffer bound to the
index buffer slot \texttt{i}. This index buffer can assign indices for
each face of the subdivision geometry that are different to the indices
of the default topology. These new indices can for example be used to
introduce additional borders into the subdivision mesh to map multiple
textures onto one subdivision geometry.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrysubdivisionmode]{rtcSetGeometrySubdivisionMode},
\hyperref[rtcinterpolate]{rtcInterpolate},
\hyperref[rtcinterpolaten]{rtcInterpolateN}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcSetGeometryDisplacementFunction}\label{rtcsetgeometrydisplacementfunction}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcSetGeometryDisplacementFunction }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ sets the displacement function}
  \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ a subdivision geometry}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCDisplacementFunctionNArguments}
\OperatorTok{\{}
  \DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ geometryUserPtr}\OperatorTok{;}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ primID}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ timeStep}\OperatorTok{;}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ u}\OperatorTok{;}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ v}\OperatorTok{;}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ Ng\_x}\OperatorTok{;}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ Ng\_y}\OperatorTok{;}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ Ng\_z}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ P\_x}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ P\_y}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ P\_z}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{;}
\OperatorTok{\};}

\KeywordTok{typedef} \DataTypeTok{void} \OperatorTok{(*}\NormalTok{RTCDisplacementFunctionN}\OperatorTok{)(}
   \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCDisplacementFunctionNArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}
\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcSetGeometryDisplacementFunction}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCDisplacementFunctionN displacement}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcSetGeometryDisplacementFunction} function registers a
displacement callback function (\texttt{displacement} argument) for the
specified subdivision geometry (\texttt{geometry} argument).

Only a single callback function can be registered per geometry, and
further invocations overwrite the previously set callback function.
Passing \texttt{NULL} as function pointer disables the registered
callback function.

The registered displacement callback function is invoked to displace
points on the subdivision geometry during spatial acceleration structure
construction, during the \texttt{rtcCommitScene} call.

The callback function of type \texttt{RTCDisplacementFunctionN} is
invoked with a number of arguments stored inside the
\texttt{RTCDisplacementFunctionNArguments} structure. The provided user
data pointer of the geometry (\texttt{geometryUserPtr} member) can be
used to point to the application's representation of the subdivision
mesh. A number \texttt{N} of points to displace are specified in a
structure of array layout. For each point to displace, the local patch
UV coordinates (\texttt{u} and \texttt{v} arrays), the normalized
geometry normal (\texttt{Ng\_x}, \texttt{Ng\_y}, and \texttt{Ng\_z}
arrays), and the position (\texttt{P\_x}, \texttt{P\_y}, and
\texttt{P\_z} arrays) are provided. The task of the displacement
function is to use this information and change the position data.

The geometry handle (\texttt{geometry} member) and primitive ID
(\texttt{primID} member) of the patch to displace are additionally
provided as well as the time step \texttt{timeStep}, which can be
important if the displacement is time-dependent and motion blur is used.

All passed arrays must be aligned to 64 bytes and properly padded to
make wide vector processing inside the displacement function easily
possible.

Also see tutorial \hyperref[displacement-geometry]{Displacement
Geometry} for an example of how to use the displacement mapping
functions.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_subdivision]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_SUBDIVISION}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcGetGeometryFirstHalfEdge}\label{rtcgetgeometryfirsthalfedge}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcGetGeometryFirstHalfEdge }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ returns the first half edge of a face}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ rtcGetGeometryFirstHalfEdge}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ faceID}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcGetGeometryFirstHalfEdge} function returns the ID of the
first half edge belonging to the specified face (\texttt{faceID}
argument). For instance in the following example the first half edge of
face \texttt{f1} is \texttt{e4}.

!\href{images/half_edges.pdf}{}

This function can only be used for subdivision geometries. As all
topologies of a subdivision geometry share the same face buffer the
function does not depend on the topology ID.

Here f0 to f7 are 8 quadrilateral faces with 4 vertices each. The edges
e0 to e23 of these faces are shown with their orientation. For each face
the ID of the edges corresponds to the slots the face occupies in the
index array of the geometry. E.g. as the indices of face f1 start at
location 4 of the index array, the first edge is edge e4, the next edge
e5, etc.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcgetgeometryfirsthalfedge]{rtcGetGeometryFirstHalfEdge},
\hyperref[rtcgetgeometryface]{rtcGetGeometryFace},
\hyperref[rtcgetgeometryoppositehalfedge]{rtcGetGeometryOppositeHalfEdge},
\hyperref[rtcgetgeometrynexthalfedge]{rtcGetGeometryNextHalfEdge},
\hyperref[rtcgetgeometryprevioushalfedge]{rtcGetGeometryPreviousHalfEdge}

This function can only be used for subdivision geometries. As all

\section{rtcGetGeometryOppositeHalfEdge}\label{rtcgetgeometryoppositehalfedge}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcGetGeometryOppositeHalfEdge }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ returns the opposite half edge}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ rtcGetGeometryOppositeHalfEdge}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ topologyID}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ edgeID}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcGetGeometryOppositeHalfEdge} function returns the ID of
the opposite half edge of the specified half edge (\texttt{edgeID}
argument) in the specified topology (\texttt{topologyID} argument). For
instance in the following example the opposite half edge of \texttt{e6}
is \texttt{e16}.

!\href{images/half_edges.pdf}{}

An opposite half edge does not exist if the specified half edge has
either no neighboring face, or more than 2 neighboring faces. In these
cases the function just returns the same edge \texttt{edgeID} again.

This function can only be used for subdivision geometries. The function
depends on the topology as the topologies of a subdivision geometry have
different index buffers assigned.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\section{rtcInterpolate}\label{rtcinterpolate}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcInterpolate }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ interpolates vertex attributes}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCInterpolateArguments}
\OperatorTok{\{}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ primID}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ u}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ v}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCBufferType bufferType}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ bufferSlot}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ P}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ dPdu}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ dPdv}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ddPdudu}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ddPdvdv}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ddPdudv}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ valueCount}\OperatorTok{;}
\OperatorTok{\};}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcInterpolate}\OperatorTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCInterpolateArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcInterpolate} function smoothly interpolates per-vertex
data over the geometry. This interpolation is supported for triangle
meshes, quad meshes, curve geometries, and subdivision geometries. Apart
from interpolating the vertex attribute itself, it is also possible to
get the first and second order derivatives of that value. This
interpolation ignores displacements of subdivision surfaces and always
interpolates the underlying base surface.

The \texttt{rtcInterpolate} call gets passed a number of arguments
inside a structure of type \texttt{RTCInterpolateArguments}. For some
geometry (\texttt{geometry} parameter) this function smoothly
interpolates the per-vertex data stored inside the specified geometry
buffer (\texttt{bufferType} and \texttt{bufferSlot} parameters) to the
u/v location (\texttt{u} and \texttt{v} parameters) of the primitive
(\texttt{primID} parameter). The number of floating point values to
interpolate and store to the destination arrays can be specified using
the \texttt{valueCount} parameter. As interpolation buffer, one can
specify vertex buffers (\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX}) and vertex
attribute buffers (\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX\_ATTRIBUTE}) as
well.

The \texttt{rtcInterpolate} call stores \texttt{valueCount} number of
interpolated floating point values to the memory location pointed to by
\texttt{P}. One can avoid storing the interpolated value by setting
\texttt{P} to \texttt{NULL}.

The first order derivative of the interpolation by u and v are stored at
the \texttt{dPdu} and \texttt{dPdv} memory locations. One can avoid
storing first order derivatives by setting both \texttt{dPdu} and
\texttt{dPdv} to \texttt{NULL}.

The second order derivatives are stored at the \texttt{ddPdudu},
\texttt{ddPdvdv}, and \texttt{ddPdudv} memory locations. One can avoid
storing second order derivatives by setting these three pointers to
\texttt{NULL}.

To use \texttt{rtcInterpolate} for a geometry, all changes to that
geometry must be properly committed using \texttt{rtcCommitGeometry}.

All input buffers and output arrays must be padded to 16 bytes, as the
implementation uses 16-byte SSE instructions to read and write into
these buffers.

See tutorial \hyperref[interpolation]{Interpolation} for an example of
using the \texttt{rtcInterpolate} function.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

For performance reasons this function does not do any error checks, thus
will not set any error flags on failure.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcinterpolaten]{rtcInterpolateN}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcInterpolateN}\label{rtcinterpolaten}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcInterpolateN }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ performs N interpolations of vertex attribute data}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCInterpolateNArguments}
\OperatorTok{\{}
\NormalTok{  RTCGeometry geometry}\OperatorTok{;}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ valid}\OperatorTok{;}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ primIDs}\OperatorTok{;}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ u}\OperatorTok{;}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ v}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCBufferType bufferType}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ bufferSlot}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ P}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ dPdu}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ dPdv}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ddPdudu}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ddPdvdv}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ddPdudv}\OperatorTok{;}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ valueCount}\OperatorTok{;}
\OperatorTok{\};}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcInterpolateN}\OperatorTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCInterpolateNArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcInterpolateN} is similar to \texttt{rtcInterpolate}, but
performs \texttt{N} many interpolations at once. It additionally gets an
array of u/v coordinates and a valid mask (\texttt{valid} parameter)
that specifies which of these coordinates are valid. The valid mask
points to \texttt{N} integers, and a value of -1 denotes valid and 0
invalid. If the valid pointer is \texttt{NULL} all elements are
considers valid. The destination arrays are filled in structure of array
(SOA) layout. The value \texttt{N} must be divisible by 4.

To use \texttt{rtcInterpolateN} for a geometry, all changes to that
geometry must be properly committed using \texttt{rtcCommitGeometry}.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

For performance reasons this function does not do any error checks, thus
will not set any error flags on failure.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcinterpolate]{rtcInterpolate}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcNewBuffer}\label{rtcnewbuffer}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcNewBuffer }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ creates a }\KeywordTok{new}\NormalTok{ data buffer}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\NormalTok{RTCBuffer rtcNewBuffer}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCDevice device}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{size\_t}\NormalTok{ byteSize}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcNewBuffer} function creates a new data buffer object of
specified size in bytes (\texttt{byteSize} argument) that is bound to
the specified device (\texttt{device} argument). The buffer object is
reference counted with an initial reference count of 1. The returned
buffer object can be released using the \texttt{rtcReleaseBuffer} API
call. The specified number of bytes are allocated at buffer construction
time and deallocated when the buffer is destroyed.

When the buffer will be used as a vertex buffer
(\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX} and
\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX\_ATTRIBUTE}), the last buffer element
must be readable using 16-byte SSE load instructions, thus padding the
last element is required for certain layouts. E.g. a standard
\texttt{float3} vertex buffer layout should add storage for at least one
more float to the end of the buffer.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure \texttt{NULL} is returned and an error code is set that can
be queried using \texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcretainbuffer]{rtcRetainBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcreleasebuffer]{rtcReleaseBuffer}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcNewSharedBuffer}\label{rtcnewsharedbuffer}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcNewSharedBuffer }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ creates a }\KeywordTok{new}\NormalTok{ shared data buffer}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\NormalTok{RTCBuffer rtcNewSharedBuffer}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCDevice device}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ptr}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{size\_t}\NormalTok{ byteSize}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcNewSharedBuffer} function creates a new shared data
buffer object bound to the specified device (\texttt{device} argument).
The buffer object is reference counted with an initial reference count
of 1. The buffer can be released using the \texttt{rtcReleaseBuffer}
function.

At construction time, the pointer to the user-managed buffer data
(\texttt{ptr} argument) including its size in bytes (\texttt{byteSize}
argument) is provided to create the buffer. At buffer construction time
no buffer data is allocated, but the buffer data provided by the
application is used. The buffer data must remain valid for as long as
the buffer may be used, and the user is responsible to free the buffer
data when no longer required.

When the buffer will be used as a vertex buffer
(\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX} and
\texttt{RTC\_BUFFER\_TYPE\_VERTEX\_ATTRIBUTE}), the last buffer element
must be readable using 16-byte SSE load instructions, thus padding the
last element is required for certain layouts. E.g. a standard
\texttt{float3} vertex buffer layout should add storage for at least one
more float to the end of the buffer.

The data pointer (\texttt{ptr} argument) must be aligned to 4 bytes;
otherwise the \texttt{rtcNewSharedBuffer} function will fail.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure \texttt{NULL} is returned and an error code is set that can
be queried using \texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcretainbuffer]{rtcRetainBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcreleasebuffer]{rtcReleaseBuffer}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcRetainBuffer}\label{rtcretainbuffer}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcRetainBuffer }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ increments the buffer reference count}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcRetainBuffer}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCBuffer buffer}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

Buffer objects are reference counted. The \texttt{rtcRetainBuffer}
function increments the reference count of the passed buffer object
(\texttt{buffer} argument). This function together with
\texttt{rtcReleaseBuffer} allows to use the internal reference counting
in a C++ wrapper class to handle the ownership of the object.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewbuffer]{rtcNewBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcreleasebuffer]{rtcReleaseBuffer}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcReleaseBuffer}\label{rtcreleasebuffer}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcReleaseBuffer }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ decrements the buffer reference count}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcReleaseBuffer}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCBuffer buffer}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

Buffer objects are reference counted. The \texttt{rtcReleaseBuffer}
function decrements the reference count of the passed buffer object
(\texttt{buffer} argument). When the reference count falls to 0, the
buffer gets destroyed.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewbuffer]{rtcNewBuffer},
\hyperref[rtcretainbuffer]{rtcRetainBuffer}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcGetBufferData}\label{rtcgetbufferdata}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcGetBufferData }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ gets a pointer to the buffer data}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rtcGetBufferData}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCBuffer buffer}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcGetBufferData} function returns a pointer to the buffer
data of the specified buffer object (\texttt{buffer} argument).

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

On failure an error code is set that can be queried using
\texttt{rtcGetDeviceError}.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcnewbuffer]{rtcNewBuffer}

\pagebreak

\section{RTCRay}\label{rtcray}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTCRay }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ single ray structure}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore\_ray.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTC\_ALIGN}\OperatorTok{(}\DecValTok{16}\OperatorTok{)}\NormalTok{ RTCRay}
\OperatorTok{\{}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ org\_x}\OperatorTok{;}        \CommentTok{// x coordinate of ray origin}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ org\_y}\OperatorTok{;}        \CommentTok{// y coordinate of ray origin}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ org\_z}\OperatorTok{;}        \CommentTok{// z coordinate of ray origin}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ tnear}\OperatorTok{;}        \CommentTok{// start of ray segment}

  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ dir\_x}\OperatorTok{;}        \CommentTok{// x coordinate of ray direction}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ dir\_y}\OperatorTok{;}        \CommentTok{// y coordinate of ray direction}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ dir\_z}\OperatorTok{;}        \CommentTok{// z coordinate of ray direction}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ time}\OperatorTok{;}         \CommentTok{// time of this ray for motion blur}

  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ tfar}\OperatorTok{;}         \CommentTok{// end of ray segment (set to hit distance)}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ mask}\OperatorTok{;}  \CommentTok{// ray mask}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ id}\OperatorTok{;}    \CommentTok{// ray ID}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ flags}\OperatorTok{;} \CommentTok{// ray flags}
\OperatorTok{\};}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{RTCRay} structure defines the ray layout for a single ray.
The ray contains the origin (\texttt{org\_x}, \texttt{org\_y},
\texttt{org\_z} members), direction vector (\texttt{dir\_x},
\texttt{dir\_y}, \texttt{dir\_z} members), and ray segment
(\texttt{tnear} and \texttt{tfar} members). The ray direction does not
have to be normalized, and only the parameter range specified by the
\texttt{tnear}/\texttt{tfar} interval is considered valid.

The ray segment must be in the range \([0, \infty]\), thus ranges that
start behind the ray origin are not allowed, but ranges can reach to
infinity.

The ray further contains a motion blur time in the range \([0, 1]\)
(\texttt{time} member), a ray mask (\texttt{mask} member), a ray ID
(\texttt{id} member), and ray flags (\texttt{flags} member). The ray
mask can be used to mask out some geometries for some rays (see
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryMask} for more details). The ray ID can be used to
identify a ray inside a callback function, even if the order of rays
inside a ray packet has changed.

The \texttt{embree4/rtcore\_ray.h} header additionally defines the same
ray structure in structure of array (SOA) layout for API functions
accepting ray packets of size 4 (\texttt{RTCRay4} type), size 8
(\texttt{RTCRay8} type), and size 16 (\texttt{RTCRay16} type). The
header additionally defines an \texttt{RTCRayNt} template for ray
packets of an arbitrary compile-time size.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtchit]{RTCHit}

\pagebreak

\section{RTCHit}\label{rtchit}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTCHit }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ single hit structure}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCHit}
\OperatorTok{\{}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ Ng\_x}\OperatorTok{;}                                        \CommentTok{// x coordinate of geometry normal}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ Ng\_y}\OperatorTok{;}                                        \CommentTok{// y coordinate of geometry normal}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ Ng\_z}\OperatorTok{;}                                        \CommentTok{// z coordinate of geometry normal}

  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ u}\OperatorTok{;}                                           \CommentTok{// barycentric u coordinate of hit}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ v}\OperatorTok{;}                                           \CommentTok{// barycentric v coordinate of hit}

  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ primID}\OperatorTok{;}                               \CommentTok{// geometry ID}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ geomID}\OperatorTok{;}                               \CommentTok{// primitive ID}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ instID}\OperatorTok{[}\NormalTok{RTC\_MAX\_INSTANCE\_LEVEL\_COUNT}\OperatorTok{];} \CommentTok{// instance ID}
\OperatorTok{\};}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{RTCHit} type defines the type of a ray/primitive
intersection result. The hit contains the unnormalized geometric normal
in object space at the hit location (\texttt{Ng\_x}, \texttt{Ng\_y},
\texttt{Ng\_z} members), the barycentric u/v coordinates of the hit
(\texttt{u} and \texttt{v} members), as well as the primitive ID
(\texttt{primID} member), geometry ID (\texttt{geomID} member), and
instance ID stack (\texttt{instID} member) of the hit. The parametric
intersection distance is not stored inside the hit, but stored inside
the \texttt{tfar} member of the ray.

The \texttt{embree4/rtcore\_ray.h} header additionally defines the same
hit structure in structure of array (SOA) layout for hit packets of size
4 (\texttt{RTCHit4} type), size 8 (\texttt{RTCHit8} type), and size 16
(\texttt{RTCHit16} type). The header additionally defines an
\texttt{RTCHitNt} template for hit packets of an arbitrary compile-time
size.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcray]{RTCRay}, {[}Multi-Level Instancing{]}

\pagebreak

\section{RTCRayHit}\label{rtcrayhit}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTCRayHit }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ combined single ray}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{hit structure}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore\_ray.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCORE\_ALIGN}\OperatorTok{(}\DecValTok{16}\OperatorTok{)}\NormalTok{ RTCRayHit}
\OperatorTok{\{}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRay ray}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCHit hit}\OperatorTok{;}
\OperatorTok{\};}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{RTCRayHit} structure is used as input for the
\texttt{rtcIntersect}-type functions and stores the ray to intersect and
some hit fields that hold the intersection result afterwards.

The \texttt{embree4/rtcore\_ray.h} header additionally defines the same
ray/hit structure in structure of array (SOA) layout for API functions
accepting ray packets of size 4 (\texttt{RTCRayHit4} type), size 8
(\texttt{RTCRayHit8} type), and size 16 (\texttt{RTCRayHit16} type). The
header additionally defines an \texttt{RTCRayHitNt} template to generate
ray/hit packets of an arbitrary compile-time size.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcray]{RTCRay}, \hyperref[rtchit]{RTCHit}

\pagebreak

\section{RTCRayN}\label{rtcrayn}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTCRayN }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ ray packet of runtime size}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore\_ray.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{;}

\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN\_org\_x}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN\_org\_y}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN\_org\_z}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN\_tnear}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN\_dir\_x}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN\_dir\_y}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN\_dir\_z}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN\_time }\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{        RTCRayN\_tfar }\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN\_mask }\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN\_id   }\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN\_flags}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

When the ray packet size is not known at compile time (e.g.~when Embree
returns a ray packet in the \texttt{RTCFilterFuncN} callback function),
Embree uses the \texttt{RTCRayN} type for ray packets. These ray packets
can only have sizes of 1, 4, 8, or 16. No other packet size will be
used.

You can either implement different special code paths for each of these
possible packet sizes and cast the ray to the appropriate ray packet
type, or implement one general code path that uses the
\texttt{RTCRayN\_XXX} helper functions to access the ray packet
components.

These helper functions get a pointer to the ray packet (\texttt{ray}
argument), the packet size (\texttt{N} argument), and returns a
reference to a component (e.g.~x-component of origin) of the the i-th
ray of the packet (\texttt{i} argument).

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtchitn]{RTCHitN}

\pagebreak

\section{RTCHitN}\label{rtchitn}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTCHitN }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ hit packet of runtime size}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ HitN}\OperatorTok{;}

\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCHitN\_Ng\_x}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ hit}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCHitN\_Ng\_y}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ hit}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCHitN\_Ng\_z}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ hit}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCHitN\_u}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ hit}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{float}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCHitN\_v}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ hit}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{unsigned}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCHitN\_primID}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ hit}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{unsigned}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCHitN\_geomID}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ hit}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{);}
\DataTypeTok{unsigned}\OperatorTok{\&}\NormalTok{ RTCHitN\_instID}\OperatorTok{(}\NormalTok{RTCHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ hit}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ i}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ level}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

When the hit packet size is not known at compile time (e.g.~when Embree
returns a hit packet in the \texttt{RTCFilterFuncN} callback function),
Embree uses the \texttt{RTCHitN} type for hit packets. These hit packets
can only have sizes of 1, 4, 8, or 16. No other packet size will be
used.

You can either implement different special code paths for each of these
possible packet sizes and cast the hit to the appropriate hit packet
type, or implement one general code path that uses the
\texttt{RTCHitN\_XXX} helper functions to access hit packet components.

These helper functions get a pointer to the hit packet (\texttt{hit}
argument), the packet size (\texttt{N} argument), and returns a
reference to a component (e.g.~x component of \texttt{Ng}) of the the
i-th hit of the packet (\texttt{i} argument).

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcrayn]{RTCRayN}

\pagebreak

\section{RTCRayHitN}\label{rtcrayhitn}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTCRayHitN }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ combined ray}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{hit packet of runtime size}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore\_ray.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayHitN}\OperatorTok{;}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ RTCRayHitN\_RayN}\OperatorTok{(}\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rayhit}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{);}
\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ RTCRayHitN\_HitN}\OperatorTok{(}\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayHitN}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rayhit}\OperatorTok{,} \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ N}\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

When the packet size of a ray/hit structure is not known at compile time
(e.g.~when Embree returns a ray/hit packet in the
\texttt{RTCIntersectFunctionN} callback function), Embree uses the
\texttt{RTCRayHitN} type for ray packets. These ray/hit packets can only
have sizes of 1, 4, 8, or 16. No other packet size will be used.

You can either implement different special code paths for each of these
possible packet sizes and cast the ray/hit to the appropriate ray/hit
packet type, or extract the \texttt{RTCRayN} and \texttt{RTCHitN}
components using the \texttt{rtcGetRayN} and \texttt{rtcGetHitN} helper
functions and use the \texttt{RTCRayN\_XXX} and \texttt{RTCHitN\_XXX}
functions to access the ray and hit parts of the structure.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtchitn]{RTCHitN}

\pagebreak

\section{RTCFeatureFlags}\label{rtcfeatureflags}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{RTCFeatureFlags }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ specifies features to enable}
  \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ ray queries}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore\_ray.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCFeatureFlags}
\OperatorTok{\{}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_NONE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{,}
  
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_MOTION\_BLUR }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_TRIANGLE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{1}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_QUAD }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{2}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_GRID }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{3}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_SUBDIVISION }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{4}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_POINT }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{...} \OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_CURVES }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{...} \OperatorTok{,}
 
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_CONE\_LINEAR\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{5}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ROUND\_LINEAR\_CURVE  }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{6}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FLAT\_LINEAR\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{7}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ROUND\_BEZIER\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{8}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FLAT\_BEZIER\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{9}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_BEZIER\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{10}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ROUND\_BSPLINE\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{11}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FLAT\_BSPLINE\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{12}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_BSPLINE\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{13}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ROUND\_HERMITE\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{14}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FLAT\_HERMITE\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{15}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_HERMITE\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{16}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ROUND\_CATMULL\_ROM\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{17}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FLAT\_CATMULL\_ROM\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{18}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_CATMULL\_ROM\_CURVE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{19}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_SPHERE\_POINT }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{20}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_DISC\_POINT }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{21}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ORIENTED\_DISC\_POINT }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{22}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ROUND\_CURVES }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{...} \OperatorTok{,}
