\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_CURVES }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{...} \OperatorTok{,}
  
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_LINEAR\_CURVES }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{...} \OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_BEZIER\_CURVES }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{...} \OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_BSPLINE\_CURVES }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{...} \OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_HERMITE\_CURVES }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{...} \OperatorTok{,}
  
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_INSTANCE }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{23}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FILTER\_FUNCTION\_IN\_ARGUMENTS }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{24}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FILTER\_FUNCTION\_IN\_GEOMETRY }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{25}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FILTER\_FUNCTION }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{...} \OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_USER\_GEOMETRY\_CALLBACK\_IN\_ARGUMENTS }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{26}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_USER\_GEOMETRY\_CALLBACK\_IN\_GEOMETRY }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{27}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_USER\_GEOMETRY }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{...} \OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_32\_BIT\_RAY\_MASK }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{\textless{}\textless{}} \DecValTok{28}\OperatorTok{,}

\NormalTok{  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ALL }\OperatorTok{=} \BaseNTok{0xffffffff}
\OperatorTok{\};}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{RTCFeatureFlags} enum specify a bit mask to enable specific
ray tracing features for ray query operations. The feature flags are
passed to the \texttt{rtcIntersect1/4/8/16} and
\texttt{rtcOccluded1/4/8/16} functions through the
\texttt{RTCIntersectArguments} and \texttt{RTCOccludedArguments}
structures. Only a ray tracing feature whose bit is enabled in the
feature mask can get used. If a feature bit is not set, the behaviour is
undefined, thus the feature may work or not. To enable multiple features
the respective features have to get combined using a bitwise \texttt{OR}
operation.

The purpose of feature flags is to reduce code size on the GPU by
enabling just the features required to render the scene. On the CPU
there is no need to use feature flags, and the default of all features
enabled (\texttt{RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ALL}) can just be kept.

The following features can get enabled using feature flags:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_MOTION\_BLUR: Enables motion blur for all geometry
  types.
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_TRIANGLE: Enables triangle geometries
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_TRIANGLE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_QUAD: Enables quad geometries
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_QUAD).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_GRID: Enables grid geometries
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_GRID).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_SUBDIVISION: Enables subdivision geometries
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_SUBDIVISION).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_POINT: Enables all point geometry types
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_XXX\_POINT)
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_CURVES: Enables all curve geometry types
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_XXX\_YYY\_CURVE)
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ROUND\_CURVES: Enables all round curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_ROUND\_XXX\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FLAT\_CURVES: Enables all flat curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_FLAT\_XXX\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_CURVES: Enables all normal
  oriented curves (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_XXX\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_LINEAR\_CURVES: Enables all linear curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_XXX\_LINEAR\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_BEZIER\_CURVES: Enables all Bézier curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_XXX\_BEZIER\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_BSPLINE\_CURVES: Enables all B-spline curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_XXX\_BSPLINE\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_HERMITE\_CURVES: Enables all Hermite curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_XXX\_HERMITE\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_CONE\_LINEAR\_CURVE: Enables cone geometry type
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_CONE\_LINEAR\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ROUND\_LINEAR\_CURVE: Enables round linear curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_ROUND\_LINEAR\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FLAT\_LINEAR\_CURVE: Enables flat linear curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_FLAT\_LINEAR\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ROUND\_BEZIER\_CURVE: Enables round Bézier curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_ROUND\_BEZIER\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FLAT\_BEZIER\_CURVE: Enables flat Bézier curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_FLAT\_BEZIER\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_BEZIER\_CURVE: Enables normal
  oriented Bézier curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_BEZIER\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ROUND\_BSPLINE\_CURVE: Enables round B-spline
  curves (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_ROUND\_BSPLINE\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FLAT\_BSPLINE\_CURVE: Enables flat B-spline curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_FLAT\_BSPLINE\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_BSPLINE\_CURVE: Enables normal
  oriented B-spline curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_BSPLINE\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ROUND\_HERMITE\_CURVE: Enables round Hermite
  curves (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_ROUND\_HERMITE\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FLAT\_HERMITE\_CURVE: Enables flat Hermite curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_FLAT\_HERMITE\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_HERMITE\_CURVE: Enables normal
  oriented Hermite curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_HERMITE\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ROUND\_CATMULL\_ROM\_CURVE: Enables round Catmull
  Rom curves (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_ROUND\_CATMULL\_ROM\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FLAT\_CATMULL\_ROM\_CURVE: Enables flat Catmull
  Rom curves (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_FLAT\_CATMULL\_ROM\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_CATMULL\_ROM\_CURVE: Enables
  normal oriented Catmull Rom curves
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_NORMAL\_ORIENTED\_CATMULL\_ROM\_CURVE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_SPHERE\_POINT: Enables sphere geometry type
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_SPHERE\_POINT).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_DISC\_POINT: Enables disc geometry type
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_DISC\_POINT).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ORIENTED\_DISC\_POINT: Enables oriented disc
  geometry types (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_ORIENTED\_DISC\_POINT).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_INSTANCE: Enables instance geometries
  (RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_INSTANCE).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FILTER\_FUNCTION\_IN\_ARGUMENTS: Enables filter
  functions passed through intersect arguments.
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FILTER\_FUNCTION\_IN\_GEOMETRY: Enable filter
  functions passed through geometry.
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_FILTER\_FUNCTION: Enables filter functions
  (argument and geometry version).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_USER\_GEOMETRY\_CALLBACK\_IN\_ARGUMENTS: Enables
  RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_USER with function pointer passed through
  intersect arguments.
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_USER\_GEOMETRY\_CALLBACK\_IN\_GEOMETRY: Enables
  RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_USER with function pointer passed through
  geometry object.
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_USER\_GEOMETRY: Enables RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_USER
  geometries (both argument and geometry callback versions).
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_32\_BIT\_RAY\_MASK: Enables full 32 bit ray masks.
  If not used, only the lower 7 bits in the ray mask are handled
  correctly.
\item
  RTC\_FEATURE\_FLAG\_ALL: Enables all features (default).
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcintersect1]{rtcIntersect1},
\hyperref[rtcintersect4816]{rtcIntersect4/8/16},
\hyperref[rtcoccluded1]{rtcOccluded1},
\hyperref[rtcoccluded4816]{rtcOccluded4/8/16},

\section{rtcInitIntersectArguments}\label{rtcinitintersectarguments}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcInitIntersectArguments }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ initializes the intersect arguments }\KeywordTok{struct}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCRayQueryFlags}
\OperatorTok{\{}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_NONE}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_INCOHERENT}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_COHERENT}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_INVOKE\_ARGUMENT\_FILTER}
\OperatorTok{\};}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCIntersectArguments}
\OperatorTok{\{}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCRayQueryFlags flags}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCFeatureFlags feature\_mask}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayQueryContext}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ context}\OperatorTok{;}
\NormalTok{  RTCFilterFunctionN filter}\OperatorTok{;}
\NormalTok{  RTCIntersectFunctionN intersect}\OperatorTok{;}
\PreprocessorTok{\#if RTC\_MIN\_WIDTH}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ minWidthDistanceFactor}\OperatorTok{;}
\PreprocessorTok{\#endif}
\OperatorTok{\};}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcInitIntersectArguments}\OperatorTok{(}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCIntersectArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcInitIntersectArguments} function initializes the optional
argument struct that can get passed to the \texttt{rtcIntersect1/4/8/16}
functions to default values. The arguments struct needs to get used for
more advanced Embree features as described here.

The \texttt{flags} member can get used to enable special traversal mode.
Using the \texttt{RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_INCOHERENT} flag uses an
optimized traversal algorithm for incoherent rays (default), while
\texttt{RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_COHERENT} uses an optimized traversal
algorithm for coherent rays (e.g.~primary camera rays).

The \texttt{feature\_mask} member should get used in SYCL to just enable
ray tracing features required to render a given scene. Please see
section \hyperref[rtcfeatureflags]{RTCFeatureFlags} for a more detailed
description.

The \texttt{context} member can get used to pass an optional
intersection context. It is guaranteed that the pointer to the context
passed to a ray query is directly passed to all callback functions. This
way it is possible to attach arbitrary data to the end of the context,
such as a per-ray payload. Please note that the ray pointer is not
guaranteed to be passed to the callback functions, thus reading
additional data from the ray pointer passed to callbacks is not
possible. See section
\hyperref[rtcinitrayquerycontext]{rtcInitRayQueryContext} for more
details.

The \texttt{filter} member specifies a filter function to invoke for
each encountered hit. The support for the argument filter function must
be enabled for a scene by using the
\texttt{RTC\_SCENE\_FLAG\_FILTER\_FUNCTION\_IN\_ARGUMENTS} scene flag.
In case of instancing this feature has to get enabled also for each
instantiated scene.

The argument filter function is invoked for each geometry for which it
got explicitely enabled using the
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryEnableFilterFunctionFromArguments} function. The
invokation of the argument filter function can also get enfored for each
geometry using the
\texttt{RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_INVOKE\_ARGUMENT\_FILTER} ray query flag.
This argument filter function is invoked as a second filter stage after
the per-geometry filter function is invoked. Only rays that passed the
first filter stage are valid in this second filter stage. Having such a
per ray-query filter function can be useful to implement modifications
of the behavior of the query, such as collecting all hits or
accumulating transparencies.

The \texttt{intersect} member specifies the user geometry callback to
get invoked for each user geometry encountered during traversal. The
user geometry callback specified this way has preference over the one
specified inside the geometry.

The \texttt{minWidthDistanceFactor} value controls the target size of
the curve radii when the min-width feature is enabled. Please see the
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrymaxradiusscale]{rtcSetGeometryMaxRadiusScale}
function for more details on the min-width feature.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

No error code is set by this function.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcintersect1]{rtcIntersect1},
\hyperref[rtcintersect4816]{rtcIntersect4/8/16},
\hyperref[rtcfeatureflags]{RTCFeatureFlags},
\hyperref[rtcinitrayquerycontext]{rtcInitRayQueryContext},
\hyperref[rtc_geometry_type_user]{RTC\_GEOMETRY\_TYPE\_USER},
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrymaxradiusscale]{rtcSetGeometryMaxRadiusScale}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcInitOccludedArguments}\label{rtcinitoccludedarguments}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcInitOccludedArguments }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ initializes the occluded arguments }\KeywordTok{struct}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCRayQueryFlags}
\OperatorTok{\{}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_NONE}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_INCOHERENT}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_COHERENT}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_INVOKE\_ARGUMENT\_FILTER}
\OperatorTok{\};}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCOccludedArguments}
\OperatorTok{\{}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCRayQueryFlags flags}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{enum}\NormalTok{ RTCFeatureFlags feature\_mask}\OperatorTok{;}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayQueryContext}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ context}\OperatorTok{;}
\NormalTok{  RTCFilterFunctionN filter}\OperatorTok{;}
\NormalTok{  RTCOccludedFunctionN intersect}\OperatorTok{;}
\PreprocessorTok{\#if RTC\_MIN\_WIDTH}
  \DataTypeTok{float}\NormalTok{ minWidthDistanceFactor}\OperatorTok{;}
\PreprocessorTok{\#endif}
\OperatorTok{\};}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcInitOccludedArguments}\OperatorTok{(}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCOccludedArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcInitOccludedArguments} function initializes the optional
argument struct that can get passed to the \texttt{rtcOccluded1/4/8/16}
functions to default values. The arguments struct needs to get used for
more advanced Embree features as described here.

The \texttt{flags} member can get used to enable special traversal mode.
Using the \texttt{RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_INCOHERENT} flag uses an
optimized traversal algorithm for incoherent rays (default), while
\texttt{RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_COHERENT} uses an optimized traversal
algorithm for coherent rays (e.g.~primary camera rays).

The \texttt{feature\_mask} member should get used in SYCL to just enable
ray tracing features required to render a given scene. Please see
section \hyperref[rtcfeatureflags]{RTCFeatureFlags} for a more detailed
description.

The \texttt{context} member can get used to pass an optional
intersection context. It is guaranteed that the pointer to the context
passed to a ray query is directly passed to all callback functions. This
way it is possible to attach arbitrary data to the end of the context,
such as a per-ray payload. Please note that the ray pointer is not
guaranteed to be passed to the callback functions, thus reading
additional data from the ray pointer passed to callbacks is not
possible. See section
\hyperref[rtcinitrayquerycontext]{rtcInitRayQueryContext} for more
details.

The \texttt{filter} member specifies a filter function to invoked for
each encountered hit. The support for the argument filter function must
be enabled for a scene by using the
\texttt{RTC\_SCENE\_FLAG\_FILTER\_FUNCTION\_IN\_ARGUMENTS} scene flag.
In case of instancing this feature has to get enabled also for each
instantiated scene.

The argument filter function is invoked for each geometry for which it
got explicitely enabled using the
\texttt{rtcSetGeometryEnableFilterFunctionFromArguments} function. The
invokation of the argument filter function can also get enfored for each
geometry using the
\texttt{RTC\_RAY\_QUERY\_FLAG\_INVOKE\_ARGUMENT\_FILTER} ray query flag.
This argument filter function is invoked as a second filter stage after
the per-geometry filter function is invoked. Only rays that passed the
first filter stage are valid in this second filter stage. Having such a
per ray-query filter function can be useful to implement modifications
of the behavior of the query, such as collecting all hits or
accumulating transparencies.

The \texttt{intersect} member specifies the user geometry callback to
get invoked for each user geometry encountered during traversal. The
user geometry callback specified this way has preference over the one
specified inside the geometry.

The \texttt{minWidthDistanceFactor} value controls the target size of
the curve radii when the min-width feature is enabled. Please see the
\hyperref[rtcsetgeometrymaxradiusscale]{rtcSetGeometryMaxRadiusScale}
function for more details on the min-width feature.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

No error code is set by this function.

\section{rtcInitRayQueryContext}\label{rtcinitrayquerycontext}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcInitRayQueryContext }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ initializes the ray query context}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayQueryContext}
\OperatorTok{\{}
  \PreprocessorTok{\#if RTC\_MAX\_INSTANCE\_LEVEL\_COUNT \textgreater{} 1}
    \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ instStackSize}\OperatorTok{;}
  \PreprocessorTok{\#endif}
  
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ instID}\OperatorTok{[}\NormalTok{RTC\_MAX\_INSTANCE\_LEVEL\_COUNT}\OperatorTok{];}
\OperatorTok{\};}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcInitRayQueryContext}\OperatorTok{(}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayQueryContext}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ context}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcInitRayQueryContext} function initializes the
intersection context to default values and should be called to
initialize every ray query context.

It is guaranteed that the pointer to the ray query context
(\texttt{RTCRayQueryContext} type) is passed to the registered callback
functions. This way it is possible to attach arbitrary data to the end
of the ray query context, such as a per-ray payload.

Inside the user geometry callback the ray query context can get used to
access the \texttt{instID} stack to know which instance the user
geometry object resides.

If not ray query context is specified when tracing a ray, a default
context is used.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

No error code is set by this function.

\hyperref[rtcintersect4816]{rtcIntersect4/8/16},
\hyperref[rtcoccluded1]{rtcOccluded1},
\hyperref[rtcoccluded4816]{rtcOccluded4/8/16}

\section{rtcIntersect1}\label{rtcintersect1}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcIntersect1 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ finds the closest hit }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ a single ray}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcIntersect1}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCScene scene}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayHit}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rayhit}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCIntersectArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ NULL}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcIntersect1} function finds the closest hit of a single
ray (\texttt{rayhit} argument) with the scene (\texttt{scene} argument).
The provided ray/hit structure contains the ray to intersect and some
hit output fields that are filled when a hit is found. The passed
optional arguments struct (\texttt{args} argument) can get used for
advanced use cases, see section
\hyperref[rtcinitintersectarguments]{rtcInitIntersectArguments} for more
details.

To trace a ray, the user has to initialize the ray origin (\texttt{org}
ray member), ray direction (\texttt{dir} ray member), ray segment
(\texttt{tnear}, \texttt{tfar} ray members), ray mask (\texttt{mask} ray
member), and set the ray flags to \texttt{0} (\texttt{flags} ray
member). The ray time (\texttt{time} ray member) must be initialized to
a value in the range \${[}0, 1{]}. The ray segment has to be in the
range \([0, \infty]\), thus ranges that start behind the ray origin are
not valid, but ranges can reach to infinity. See Section
\hyperref[rtcray]{RTCRay} for the ray layout description.

The geometry ID (\texttt{geomID} hit member) of the hit data must be
initialized to \texttt{RTC\_INVALID\_GEOMETRY\_ID} (-1).

When no intersection is found, the ray/hit data is not updated. When an
intersection is found, the hit distance is written into the
\texttt{tfar} member of the ray and all hit data is set, such as
unnormalized geometry normal in object space (\texttt{Ng} hit member),
local hit coordinates (\texttt{u}, \texttt{v} hit member), instance ID
stack (\texttt{instID} hit member), geometry ID (\texttt{geomID} hit
member), and primitive ID (\texttt{primID} hit member). See Section
\hyperref[rtchit]{RTCHit} for the hit layout description.

If the instance ID stack has a prefix of values not equal to
\texttt{RTC\_INVALID\_GEOMETRY\_ID}, the instance ID on each level
corresponds to the geometry ID of the hit instance of the higher-level
scene, the geometry ID corresponds to the hit geometry inside the hit
instanced scene, and the primitive ID corresponds to the n-th primitive
of that geometry.

If level 0 of the instance ID stack is equal to
\texttt{RTC\_INVALID\_GEOMETRY\_ID}, the geometry ID corresponds to the
hit geometry inside the top-level scene, and the primitive ID
corresponds to the n-th primitive of that geometry.

The implementation makes no guarantees that primitives whose hit
distance is exactly at (or very close to) \texttt{tnear} or
\texttt{tfar} are hit or missed. If you want to exclude intersections at
\texttt{tnear} just pass a slightly enlarged \texttt{tnear}, and if you
want to include intersections at \texttt{tfar} pass a slightly enlarged
\texttt{tfar}.

The ray pointer passed to callback functions is not guaranteed to be
identical to the original ray provided. To extend the ray with
additional data to be accessed in callback functions, use the ray query
context. See section
\hyperref[rtcinitrayquerycontext]{rtcInitRayQueryContext} for more
details.

The ray/hit structure must be aligned to 16 bytes.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

For performance reasons this function does not do any error checks, thus
will not set any error flags on failure.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcoccluded1]{rtcOccluded1},
\hyperref[rtcintersect4816]{rtcIntersect4/8/16},
\hyperref[rtcrayhit]{RTCRayHit},
\hyperref[rtcinitintersectarguments]{rtcInitIntersectArguments}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcOccluded1}\label{rtcoccluded1}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcOccluded1 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ finds any hit }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ a single ray}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcOccluded1}\OperatorTok{(}
\NormalTok{  RTCScene scene}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRay}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCOccludedArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ NULL}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcOccluded1} function checks for a single ray (\texttt{ray}
argument) whether there is any hit with the scene (\texttt{scene}
argument). The passed optional arguments struct (\texttt{args} argument)
can get used for advanced use cases, see section
\hyperref[rtcinitoccludedarguments]{rtcInitOccludedArguments} for more
details.

To trace a ray, the user must initialize the ray origin (\texttt{org}
ray member), ray direction (\texttt{dir} ray member), ray segment
(\texttt{tnear}, \texttt{tfar} ray members), ray mask (\texttt{mask} ray
member), and must set the ray flags to \texttt{0} (\texttt{flags} ray
member). The ray time (\texttt{time} ray member) must be initialized to
a value in the range \([0, 1]\). The ray segment must be in the range
\([0, \infty]\), thus ranges that start behind the ray origin are not
valid, but ranges can reach to infinity. See Section
\hyperref[rtcray]{RTCRay} for the ray layout description.

When no intersection is found, the ray data is not updated. In case a
hit was found, the \texttt{tfar} component of the ray is set to
\texttt{-inf}.

The implementation makes no guarantees that primitives whose hit
distance is exactly at (or very close to) \texttt{tnear} or
\texttt{tfar} are hit or missed. If you want to exclude intersections at
\texttt{tnear} just pass a slightly enlarged \texttt{tnear}, and if you
want to include intersections at \texttt{tfar} pass a slightly enlarged
\texttt{tfar}.

The ray pointer passed to callback functions is not guaranteed to be
identical to the original ray provided. To extend the ray with
additional data to be accessed in callback functions, use the ray query
context. See section
\hyperref[rtcinitrayquerycontext]{rtcInitRayQueryContext} for more
details.

The ray must be aligned to 16 bytes.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

For performance reasons this function does not do any error checks, thus
will not set any error flags on failure.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcintersect1]{rtcIntersect1},
\hyperref[rtcoccluded4816]{rtcOccluded4/8/16},
\hyperref[rtcray]{RTCRay},
\hyperref[rtcinitoccludedarguments]{rtcInitOccludedArguments}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcIntersect4/8/16}\label{rtcintersect4816}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcIntersect4}\OperatorTok{/}\DecValTok{8}\OperatorTok{/}\DecValTok{16} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ finds the closest hits }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ a ray packet}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcIntersect4}\OperatorTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ valid}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCScene scene}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayHit4}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rayhit}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCIntersectArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ NULL}
\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcIntersect8}\OperatorTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ valid}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCScene scene}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayHit8}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rayhit}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCIntersectArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ NULL}
\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcIntersect16}\OperatorTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ valid}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCScene scene}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRayHit16}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rayhit}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCIntersectArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ NULL}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcIntersect4/8/16} functions finds the closest hits for a
ray packet of size 4, 8, or 16 (\texttt{rayhit} argument) with the scene
(\texttt{scene} argument). The ray/hit input contains a ray packet and
hit packet. The passed optional arguments struct (\texttt{args}
argument) are used to pass additional arguments for advanced features.
See Section \hyperref[rtcintersect1]{rtcIntersect1} for more details and
a description of how to set up and trace rays.

A ray valid mask must be provided (\texttt{valid} argument) which stores
one 32-bit integer (\texttt{-1} means valid and \texttt{0} invalid) per
ray in the packet. Only active rays are processed, and hit data of
inactive rays is not changed.

The ray pointer passed to callback functions is not guaranteed to be
identical to the original ray provided. To extend the ray with
additional data to be accessed in callback functions, use the ray query
context. See section
\hyperref[rtcinitrayquerycontext]{rtcInitRayQueryContext} for more
details.

For \texttt{rtcIntersect4} the ray packet must be aligned to 16 bytes,
for \texttt{rtcIntersect8} the alignment must be 32 bytes, and for
\texttt{rtcIntersect16} the alignment must be 64 bytes.

The \texttt{rtcIntersect4}, \texttt{rtcIntersect8} and
\texttt{rtcIntersect16} functions may change the ray packet size and ray
order when calling back into filter functions or user geometry
callbacks. Under some conditions the application can assume packets to
stay intakt, which can determined by querying the
\texttt{RTC\_DEVICE\_PROPERTY\_NATIVE\_RAY4\_SUPPORTED},
\texttt{RTC\_DEVICE\_PROPERTY\_NATIVE\_RAY8\_SUPPORTED},
\texttt{RTC\_DEVICE\_PROPERTY\_NATIVE\_RAY16\_SUPPORTED} properties
through the \texttt{rtcGetDeviceProperty} function. See
\hyperref[rtcgetdeviceproperty]{rtcGetDeviceProperty} for more
information.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

For performance reasons this function does not do any error checks, thus
will not set any error flags on failure.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcintersect1]{rtcIntersect1},
\hyperref[rtcoccluded4816]{rtcOccluded4/8/16},
\hyperref[rtcinitintersectarguments]{rtcInitIntersectArguments}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcOccluded4/8/16}\label{rtcoccluded4816}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcOccluded4}\OperatorTok{/}\DecValTok{8}\OperatorTok{/}\DecValTok{16} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ finds any hits }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ a ray packet}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcOccluded4}\OperatorTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ valid}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCScene scene}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRay4}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCOccludedArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ NULL}
\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcOccluded8}\OperatorTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ valid}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCScene scene}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRay8}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCOccludedArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ NULL}
\OperatorTok{);}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcOccluded16}\OperatorTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \DataTypeTok{int}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ valid}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCScene scene}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRay16}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCOccludedArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ NULL}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcOccluded4/8/16} functions checks for each active ray of
the ray packet of size 4, 8, or 16 (\texttt{ray} argument) whether there
is any hit with the scene (\texttt{scene} argument). The passed optional
arguments struct (\texttt{args} argument) can get used for advanced use
cases, see section
\hyperref[rtcinitoccludedarguments]{rtcInitOccludedArguments} for more
details. See Section \hyperref[rtcoccluded1]{rtcOccluded1} for more
details and a description of how to set up and trace occlusion rays.

A ray valid mask must be provided (\texttt{valid} argument) which stores
one 32-bit integer (\texttt{-1} means valid and \texttt{0} invalid) per
ray in the packet. Only active rays are processed, and hit data of
inactive rays is not changed.

The ray pointer passed to callback functions is not guaranteed to be
identical to the original ray provided. To extend the ray with
additional data to be accessed in callback functions, use the ray query
context. See section
\hyperref[rtcinitrayquerycontext]{rtcInitRayQueryContext} for more
details.

For \texttt{rtcOccluded4} the ray packet must be aligned to 16 bytes,
for \texttt{rtcOccluded8} the alignment must be 32 bytes, and for
\texttt{rtcOccluded16} the alignment must be 64 bytes.

The \texttt{rtcOccluded4}, \texttt{rtcOccluded8} and
\texttt{rtcOccluded16} functions may change the ray packet size and ray
order when calling back into intersect filter functions or user geometry
callbacks. Under some conditions the application can assume packets to
stay intakt, which can determined by querying the
\texttt{RTC\_DEVICE\_PROPERTY\_NATIVE\_RAY4\_SUPPORTED},
\texttt{RTC\_DEVICE\_PROPERTY\_NATIVE\_RAY8\_SUPPORTED},
\texttt{RTC\_DEVICE\_PROPERTY\_NATIVE\_RAY16\_SUPPORTED} properties
through the \texttt{rtcGetDeviceProperty} function. See
\hyperref[rtcgetdeviceproperty]{rtcGetDeviceProperty} for more
information.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

For performance reasons this function does not do any error checks, thus
will not set any error flags on failure.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcoccluded1]{rtcOccluded1},
\hyperref[rtcintersect4816]{rtcIntersect4/8/16},
\hyperref[rtcinitoccludedarguments]{rtcInitOccludedArguments}

\pagebreak

\section{rtcForwardIntersect1}\label{rtcforwardintersect1}

\subsubsection{NAME}\label{name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rtcForwardIntersect1 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ forwards a single ray to }\KeywordTok{new}\NormalTok{ scene}
\NormalTok{  from user geometry callback}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{SYNOPSIS}\label{synopsis}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\PreprocessorTok{\#include }\ImportTok{\textless{}embree4/rtcore.h\textgreater{}}

\DataTypeTok{void}\NormalTok{ rtcForwardIntersect1}\OperatorTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{const} \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCIntersectFunctionNArguments}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ args}\OperatorTok{,}
\NormalTok{  RTCScene scene}\OperatorTok{,}
  \KeywordTok{struct}\NormalTok{ RTCRay}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ray}\OperatorTok{,}
  \DataTypeTok{unsigned} \DataTypeTok{int}\NormalTok{ instID}
\OperatorTok{);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{DESCRIPTION}\label{description}

The \texttt{rtcForwardIntersect1} function forwards the traversal of a
transformed ray (\texttt{ray} argument) into a scene (\texttt{scene}
argument) from a user geometry callback. The function can only get
invoked from a user geometry callback for a ray traversal initiated with
the \texttt{rtcIntersect1} function. The callback arguments structure of
the callback invokation has to get passed to the ray forwarding
(\texttt{args} argument). The user geometry callback should instantly
terminate after invoking the \texttt{rtcForwardIntersect1} function.

Only the ray origin and ray direction members of the ray argument are
used for forwarding, all additional ray properties are inherited from
the initial ray traversal invokation of \texttt{rtcIntersect1}.

The implementation of the \texttt{rtcForwardIntersect1} function
recursively continues the ray traversal into the specified scene and
pushes the provided instance ID (\texttt{instID} argument) to the
instance ID stack. Hit information is updated into the ray hit structure
passed to the original \texttt{rtcIntersect1} invokation.

This function can get used to implement user defined instancing using
user geometries, e.g.~by transforming the ray in a special way, and/or
selecting between different scenes to instantiate.

When using Embree on the CPU it is possible to recursively invoke
\texttt{rtcIntersect1} directly from a user geometry callback. However,
when SYCL is used, recursively tracing rays is not directly supported,
and the \texttt{rtcForwardIntersect1} function must be used.

The ray structure must be aligned to 16 bytes.

\subsubsection{EXIT STATUS}\label{exit-status}

For performance reasons this function does not do any error checks, thus
will not set any error flags on failure.

\subsubsection{SEE ALSO}\label{see-also}

\hyperref[rtcintersect1]{rtcIntersect1}, \hyperref[rtcray]{RTCRay}

\pagebreak

\OperatorTok{);}
